# AMC Scoring Methodology

## Public Methodology Version

AMC publishes a machine-readable scoring methodology manifest so reports, badges, API integrations, and auditors can pin exactly which scoring rules were used.

| Field | Current Value |
|-------|---------------|
| Methodology ID | `amc-public-scoring-methodology` |
| Methodology Version | `2026.06.19-r208` |
| Release Date | `2026-06-19` |
| Manifest Command | `amc methodology --json` |
| Human-Readable Command | `amc methodology` |
| Sample Dataset Command | `amc methodology --sample-dataset --json` |

Every generated diagnostic report now includes a `methodology` object with the methodology id, version, release date, question-set summary, public documentation link, and SHA-256 hash of the canonical manifest. Reports also include a `methodologyVersioning` receipt that binds public comparability to changelog, deprecation, migration, telemetry, benchmark, calibration, archive, export-sanitization, and badge-assurance proof. Badge URLs include `amc_methodology`, `amc_methodology_hash`, and `amc_methodology_assurance` query parameters, and badge HTML/Markdown includes a title containing the same method reference.

## Reproducibility Packet

Researchers and reviewers can export the public scoring instrument directly from the compiled CLI:

```bash
amc methodology --reproducibility --json
amc methodology --reproducibility --format markdown --out amc-methodology-reproducibility.md
```

The packet includes the methodology id, version, content hash, source paths, scoring formulas, evidence trust tiers, full default diagnostic question inventory, level descriptors, evidence gates, question-bank SHA-256, reproduction commands, limitations, and external review alignment. It is intended to make the scoring instrument reviewable without relying on whitepaper prose alone. It does not create a DOI or arXiv identifier and does not publish private customer evidence or historical case-study datasets.

## Public Sample Case-Study Dataset

Researchers can also export a small public sample case-study dataset that exercises the methodology row shape without exposing private customer evidence:

```bash
amc methodology --sample-dataset --json
amc methodology --sample-dataset --format markdown --out amc-methodology-case-studies.md
```

The dataset includes six synthetic L0-L5 case rows, a dataset-card style metadata block, methodology and question-bank hashes, intended/out-of-scope uses, privacy notes, source references, reproduction commands, and limitations. It is intended for parser tests, tutorials, methodology review, and examples of how evidence profiles and layer scores can be represented. It is not empirical validation, a real customer dataset, a third-party benchmark, a DOI/arXiv publication, or proof that AMC's whitepaper case studies are externally verified.

The methodology hash changes when AMC changes scoring formulas, trust-tier semantics, question-set bindings, report bindings, benchmark versioning rules, or published limitations. ## External Source Verification Policy

AMC requires live or primary-source verification before external source-backed claims can be used as public score, benchmark, or methodology evidence. Repository search metadata, cached snippets, local corpus fields, unavailable source summaries, and stale source descriptions can seed triage, but they cannot establish parity, benchmark compatibility, source-backed methodology changes, or external proof.

Accepted source statuses are:

| Status | Meaning |
|--------|---------|
| `live_verified_primary_source` | The source was retrieved live from a primary location during the review. |
| `primary_source_verified_with_retrieval_date` | A primary source was verified and the retrieval date is recorded. |
| `source_unavailable_disclosed` | The source could not be retrieved; the attempted URL/date are recorded and source-specific parity claims are avoided. |
| `metadata_only_rejected` | Metadata or cached snippets were available, but they were not accepted as proof. |

When a source is unavailable, AMC may still implement source-independent methodology safeguards, but the unavailable source must be disclosed. AMC source review may use high-level behavioral signals and public facts, but must not copy third-party code, commands, prompts, datasets, examples, UI text/assets, README prose, screenshots, benchmark rows, configuration, or implementation details unless a separate license review explicitly allows it.

GBQA-style agent quality-assurance benchmark rows are relevant to AMC only when they map to existing Score/Shield/Watch metric-validity receipts: live repository/license/default-branch proof, agent-harness or task-corpus manifests, quality/reward criteria, test-oracle evidence, report/schema artifacts, CI regression receipts, metric owner, confidence interval, signed evidence, and row hashes. AMC must not create a standalone source-specific subsystem for GBQA-style rows; metadata-only or copied-source claims remain invalid public evidence.

## Deprecation Notice

Reports and badges that do not include `methodology.id`, `methodology.version`, `methodology.hash`, and `questionSet.version` are legacy outputs and should not be used as current external proof.

## Migration Guidance

For a current proof bundle:

- Run `amc methodology --json` and store the manifest hash with audit evidence.
- Export `amc methodology --sample-dataset --json` when a public sample case-study row shape is needed without exposing customer evidence.
- Regenerate diagnostic reports so the methodology block is present in JSON and Markdown outputs.
- Regenerate badges so `amc_methodology`, `amc_methodology_hash`, and `amc_methodology_assurance` are present in badge URLs and titles.
- Regenerate benchmark receipts when corpus, harness, model-pool, tier-policy, verification protocol, scoring formula, or cost-accounting versions change.
- Regenerate or dual-run tournament receipts when opponent pools, round counts, seed policy, generation counts, replay evidence, ranking aggregation, or leaderboard publication rules change.
- For historical reports, preserve the original artifact and attach a fresh AMC report instead of editing the old report in place.

## Evaluation Mode Taxonomy

AMC reports, badges, benchmark receipts, and Watch alerts can all be evidence-bearing artifacts, but they answer different questions. The public methodology now exposes an evaluation-mode taxonomy so users do not treat maturity scores, replay receipts, drift alerts, provider canaries, and security guardrail receipts as interchangeable proof.

| `provider_drift` | Watch; API | Provider/model canary comparisons across versions, capability slices, evaluator-library proof, observability-pipeline proof, orbit-monitor source/leaderboard proof, geospatial tool-calling proof, Evidra evidence-chain proof, and evaluator reliability signals. | Provider drift benchmark eval-pack rows, evaluator-framework proof fields, observability pipeline provenance fields, orbit-monitor source/leaderboard/reload proof fields, geospatial task/dataset/tool/trace/judge proof fields, Evidra source/protocol/evidence-chain proof fields, and Watch alert projections | Disclose canary family, evaluator framework/version, observability pipeline, orbit-monitor source/leaderboard context when used, geospatial benchmark identity when used, Evidra provider-route and protocol context when used, metric suite, sample size, and waiver state before procurement or compliance use. |

For `metric_validation`, RAG chunking technique proof, Kubernetes operational-agent proof, SecureVibeBench secure-coding proof, Parallel/OpenClaw research-skill, and resume-RAG evaluator receipts are optional source-backed proof bindings. RAG chunking technique proof binds IBM/rag-chunking-techniques-style source/license/default-branch/README/policy-corpus/simple-RAG-notebook/smart-chunking-notebook/RAG-evaluation-notebook/chunking-strategy/retrieval-pipeline/embedding-vectorstore/evaluation-dataset/metric/CI proof into `diagnostic.metricValidation.rows[]` and `diagnostic.metricValidation.evalPack.rows[]`. Kubernetes operational-agent proof binds hariohmprasath/k8s-ai-style source/license/default-branch/README/release/build-workflow/agent-module/MCP-server/Kubernetes-tool-inventory/diagnostic/resource/log-analysis/metric/CI proof into `diagnostic.metricValidation.rows[]` and `diagnostic.metricValidation.evalPack.rows[]`. SecureVibeBench proof binds iCSawyer/SecureVibeBench-style source/license/homepage/default-branch/README/results/dataset/format/evaluation-runner/agent-adapter/vulnerability-scenario/test-script/parser/patch-diff/metric/CI proof into `diagnostic.metricValidation.rows[]` and `diagnostic.metricValidation.evalPack.rows[]`. For public methodology binding, Sutro-style batch methodology assurance is a source-backed proof binding for function/schema, data-source, input-order, priority, dry-run cost, model-pool, observability, result export, retention, multi-model, and embedding semantics. Agent Belt methodology assurance is a source-backed proof binding for source/license/release snapshots, README/docs, scenario schema and manifests, agent adapters, custom-agent contract, workspace-diff and rule checks, multi-judge consensus, per-turn judging, pass@k/pass^k reliability, worktree/Docker isolation, export formats, CI workflows, package digests, signed evidence, and row hashes. Critic Rubrics methodology assurance is a source-backed proof binding for no-license source snapshots, arXiv version, release tags, rubric feature taxonomy, type-safe function-calling schema, sparse outcome proxy, reranking and early-stopping metric reports, signed evidence, and row hashes. Awesome AI Evaluation Guide methodology assurance is a source-backed proof binding for hparreao/Awesome-AI-Evaluation-Guide source/license/default-branch snapshots, README guide manifests, benchmark guides, tools/platforms guides, metric-selection taxonomies, thresholds, calibration, component traces, human-in-loop review, cost controls, deprecation notices, migration guidance, signed evidence, and row hashes. When resume-RAG evaluator proof is supplied, proof binding includes source/license boundaries, resume upload/parser/job-description manifests, RAG strategy, query expansion, retrieval config, vector-store, Ollama and embedding model ids, evaluation endpoint ids, candidate-rating scale, batch mode, privacy boundary, dependency lock, owner, sample-size, confidence-interval, signed evidence, and row hashes.

For `benchmark_replay`, AI-agent benchmark comparison receipts are optional source-backed proof bindings. When supplied, proof binding includes source/repository/license refs, agent roster, benchmark dataset, source manifest, pricing snapshot, user-report manifest, leaderboard and score manifests, eval-pack manifest, fixture hash, replay command, result manifest, score-delta report, CI receipt, coverage metrics, signed evidence, and row hashes. JudgeIt-style LLM-as-judge receipts are optional source-backed proof bindings. When supplied, the public meaning extends to RAG, multi-turn query-rewrite, Text2SQL, and agentic workflow replay claims, and proof binding includes repository, dataset, golden-text, generated-text, pipeline, judge-model, judge-rubric, human-eval, evaluation, batch, export, metric, replay, signed-evidence, and row-hash receipts. BenchLoop-style local benchmark receipts are optional source-backed proof bindings for repeatable local-first LLM benchmark claims; when supplied, proof binding includes repository/package snapshots, suite/task/frozen-task/scorer manifests, harness/provider/endpoint/model configs, machine profile, dependency lock, run config, output manifest, metrics report, agent-loop/tool-call/token-latency traces, run persistence, export, replay command, deterministic seed, local quality/speed/reliability/agent metrics, signed-evidence, and row-hash receipts. FreshStack-style IR/RAG retrieval receipts are also optional source-backed proof bindings; when supplied, public meaning extends to versioned technical retrieval benchmarks over StackOverflow-style queries and GitHub-derived corpora, and proof binding includes repository, paper, query/corpus dataset, license, BEIR, qrels, chunking, retriever, index, runfile, evaluator, metric, leaderboard, replay, signed-evidence, and row-hash receipts. DB context enrichment receipts are optional source-backed proof bindings for context-engineered natural-language-to-SQL claims; when supplied, proof binding includes repository, extension manifest, schema discovery, ContextSet, templates, facets, value searches, golden datasets, Evalbench and LLM-rater configs, failure-case, hill-climb, mutation, final-validation, replay, signed-evidence, and row-hash receipts.

## Benchmark Methodology Versioning

Static offline benchmark evidence and live dynamic execution evidence answer different questions. AMC publishes version fields for both so users can tell whether a benchmark receipt is comparable to a prior run or only useful as a fresh baseline.

Required audit fields:

- `corpusVersion`
- `harnessVersion`
- `modelPoolVersion`
- `tierPolicyVersion`
- `verificationProtocolVersion`
- `scoringFormulaVersion`
- `costAccountingVersion`
- `telemetrySchemaVersion`
- `calibrationProtocolVersion`

| Track | Public meaning | Proof binding | Comparability rule |
|-------|----------------|---------------|--------------------|
| `static_offline` | Fast replay or label-based benchmark evidence used for iteration, regression gates, and offline comparison. | Benchmark manifests, metric-validation eval packs, provider-drift eval packs, and row hashes. | Compare only when corpus, harness, model-pool, tier-policy, verification-protocol, scoring, and cost-accounting versions match or a migration note is attached. |
| `live_dynamic` | End-to-end execution evidence from a running agent, provider, tool, or environment window. | Live drift receipts, Watch alerts, signed runtime evidence, and Shield verification receipts. | Do not substitute for static offline evidence; compare only with matching environment, provider/model, cost-accounting, and evidence-window declarations. |

Version-change triggers:

| Change trigger | Version impact | Migration |
|----------------|----------------|-----------|
| `corpus_or_question_set_change` | Increment corpus or question-set version and publish methodology migration guidance when public score semantics change. | Regenerate affected reports before external comparison. |
| `harness_or_environment_change` | Increment harness version and bind the environment snapshot to benchmark and live receipts. | Attach a waiver or rerun old and new harnesses side by side before claiming trend continuity. |
| `model_pool_or_tier_policy_change` | Increment model-pool or tier-policy version because cost, capability, and routing labels are no longer directly comparable. | Publish the old and new pool/tier mapping hashes and regenerate provider or replay receipts. |
| `verification_protocol_or_scoring_change` | Increment methodology version when pass predicates, evaluator gates, score formulas, or cost accounting change. | Preserve historical artifacts and create a fresh report or benchmark receipt under the new methodology. |
| `metronous_telemetry_calibration_methodology_change` | Increment methodology, telemetry-schema, benchmark-corpus, threshold-policy, calibration-protocol, export-sanitization, and badge-assurance versions when Metronous-style local telemetry, benchmark aggregation, model calibration, archive, cost, or report-binding semantics change. | Regenerate badges and reports before comparing kiosvantra/metronous-style, local AI agent telemetry, benchmark, threshold, model calibration, OpenCode-agent, local archive, or methodology-versioning claims. |
| `tournament_or_leaderboard_protocol_change` | Increment harness, verification-protocol, and scoring versions when tournament format, opponent pool, generation count, seed policy, ranking aggregation, replay protocol, or leaderboard publication rules change. | Rerun or dual-run the old and new tournament protocols before comparing leaderboard positions, strategy-coding rankings, or iterative-learning deltas. |
| `multimodal_rag_corpus_or_metric_change` | Increment corpus, verification-protocol, scoring, and methodology versions when multimodal RAG text/image collection, extraction or filtering, modality representation, retrieval or element-selection protocol, output-image policy, evaluator rubric, judge model, or text/image metric definitions change. | Dual-run old and new modality coverage and regenerate badges or reports before comparing M2RAG-style mixed text/image scores. |
| `rag_audit_dataset_or_diagnosis_change` | Increment corpus, verification-protocol, scoring, and methodology versions when RAG audit QA generation, support-span grounding, endpoint contract, judge model, detailed metric definitions, failure-diagnosis taxonomy, retriever/generator attribution, privacy mode, MCP/server telemetry boundary, report export, or threshold semantics change. | Regenerate badges or reports before comparing RagScore-style RAG audit, generated-QA, detailed-metric, failure-diagnosis, or privacy/local-cloud RAG evaluation claims. |
| `soc_dataset_schema_or_label_quality_change` | Increment corpus, verification-protocol, scoring, and methodology versions when SOC dataset source corpus, LAB/REAL split, conversion script, labeling prompt, output schema, ATT&CK mapping, action schema, validation, label-quality, cross-model audit, license boundary, replay pass-rate, or threshold semantics change. | Regenerate badges or reports before comparing AD-GEN-style SOC dataset replay, ATT&CK narrative, LLM SOC automation, validated synthetic label, or endpoint telemetry benchmark claims. |
| `legal_code_rag_metric_validity_change` | Increment corpus, verification-protocol, scoring, and methodology versions when Legal Code RAG corpus/source boundaries, Legifrance ingestion, vector database, embedding model, windowing, hybrid-search, query-rewrite, routing, evaluation dataset, reference-answer, evaluator, metric, owner, or sample/CI semantics change. | Regenerate badges or reports before comparing HamzaG737/legal-code-rag-style, Legal Code RAG, French legal-code RAG, Legifrance-backed RAG, Qdrant-backed RAG, windowing, hybrid-search, query-rewrite, routing, or legal RAG metric-validity claims. |

## Methodology Versioning Assurance

AMC emits a methodology-versioning receipt beside each diagnostic report so a report or badge can be compared only when the public methodology, question set, changelog, deprecation notice, migration guidance, and source-backed telemetry/calibration assumptions are pinned.

- ID: `metronous-telemetry-calibration-methodology-assurance`
- Source reference: `github:kiosvantra/metronous`
- Source pattern: `metronous_local_telemetry_benchmark_calibration`
- Required audit fields: `methodologyId`, `methodologyVersion`, `methodologyHash`, `questionSetVersion`, `changelogEntryHash`, `deprecationNoticeHash`, `migrationGuidanceHash`, `telemetrySchemaHash`, `benchmarkCorpusHash`, `thresholdPolicyHash`, `modelCalibrationReportHash`, `costAccountingPolicyHash`, `localArchiveManifestHash`, `exportSanitizationPolicyHash`, `badgeQueryParamHash`, `diagnosticReceiptHash`
- Badge query params: `amc_methodology`, `amc_methodology_hash`, `amc_methodology_assurance`
- Diagnostic fields: `methodology`, `methodologyVersioning`, `methodologyVersioning.receiptHash`
- Fail-closed rule: reports and badges fail methodology-versioning assurance when the methodology hash, current changelog row, migration guidance, deprecation notice, telemetry schema, benchmark corpus, threshold policy, model calibration report, cost accounting, local archive, export sanitization, or badge assurance hash is missing.
- No-copy boundary: AMC may use kiosvantra/metronous only as a high-level local telemetry, benchmark, threshold, archive, and model-calibration signal. AMC does not copy Metronous code, commands, configs, threshold values, docs, README prose, UI text/assets, plugin implementation, screenshots, database schemas, telemetry payloads, or benchmark examples.

- No-copy boundary: AMC may use sutro-sh/sutro only as a high-level unstructured-data batch inference, grounded judge/classifier/extractor, observability, result-export, retention, and cost-estimation methodology signal. AMC does not copy Sutro code, commands, SDK snippets, config files, README prose, docs prose, UI text/assets, screenshots, data samples, result previews, templates, package metadata, or implementation details.

AMC does not copy repository code, commands, configs, docs prose, README prose, scripts, rubrics, examples, tests, model outputs, data, benchmark rows, metric values, release text, or implementation details.

## Score Claim Boundaries

Public score claims need explicit boundaries when a capability name implies more than the underlying evidence proves. AMC now publishes the following boundaries in the methodology manifest.

| Boundary | Applies when | Public disclosure | Required evidence | Migration |
|----------|--------------|-------------------|-------------------|-----------|
| `cryptography_benchmark_methodology_integrity` | A report, badge, benchmark receipt, or public claim uses cryptography capability, AICrypto-style benchmark, MCQ, CTF, proof, vulnerability exploitation, formal reasoning, or expert-baseline evidence as external proof of agent or model maturity. | Crypto benchmark labels, aggregate leaderboard scores, model rankings, local run outputs, CTF solve counts, proof scores, MCQ accuracy, website figures, or copied task examples do not establish comparable cryptography capability without versioned task-family, scoring, dataset, expert-baseline, sandbox, and proof-rubric evidence. | Signed paper/arXiv version, repository snapshot hash, dataset release and license refs, task-family manifest, MCQ manifest and answer-key/rubric proof, CTF challenge manifest, CTF sandbox/toolchain and automated-agent-framework proof, proof-problem manifest, proof rubric/reference-solution proof, human expert baseline reference, model/provider config, run/output artifact, scoring formula, per-family counts, contamination or recency policy, regression thresholds, source refs, signed evidence refs, and row hashes. | Regenerate or relabel reports generated under `2026.06.13-r93` before using AICrypto-style, cryptography-capability benchmark, MCQ, CTF, proof-problem, vulnerability-exploitation, formal-reasoning, or expert-baseline claims. |
| `divergent_trajectory_reasoning` | A report, badge, benchmark receipt, or public claim describes an agent as creative, divergent, multi-path, resilient under dead-ends, or capable of unconventional problem solving. | Success-only or single-path completion evidence does not establish divergent trajectory reasoning. | Signed trajectory evidence that separates completed paths from off-path attempts and records multiple mechanism-distinct paths or rejected mechanisms. | Relabel success-only historical claims or regenerate reports with trajectory-divergence evidence before using divergent-capability language. |
| `social_simulation_realism` | A report, badge, benchmark receipt, or public claim describes synthetic agents as realistic, human-like, community-representative, or faithful proxies for public discourse. | Plausible or coherent synthetic responses do not establish distributional realism for social simulation. | Matched empirical baseline evidence with signed distribution checks for harm prevalence, sentiment or affect, semantic alignment, lexical diversity, population/context labels, and detector limitations. | Relabel plausibility-only historical claims or regenerate reports with matched empirical distribution checks before using social-realism language. |
| `persona_policy_realism` | A report, badge, benchmark receipt, or public claim describes simulated users as robust, human-like, task-preserving personas, or representative of challenging user behavior. | A cooperative simulator, hand-written persona prompt, or stable task score does not establish robust persona-policy realism. | Signed persona-policy evidence for policy identity, diversity cluster coverage, human-likeness, behavior coverage, task-goal preservation, and baseline-to-live persona distribution checks. | Regenerate or relabel reports generated under `2026.06.13-r13` before using persona-policy realism, human-like user simulation, or robust persona benchmark claims. |
| `ctf_live_evaluation_integrity` | A report, badge, benchmark receipt, or public claim uses CTF or flag-solving results as proof of live cybersecurity-agent capability. | Static, reused, or score-only CTF evidence does not establish uncontaminated live cybersecurity-agent capability. | Signed live CTF evidence for event and challenge context, agent instance and team-account boundaries, flag acceptance, first-correct-flag forwarding, external-search contamination risk, competition-impact risk, and per-agent independence. | Regenerate or relabel reports generated under `2026.06.13-r15` before using live CTF, cybersecurity benchmark, or flag-solving claims as external proof. |

## Metric Validity and Reliability Checks

Each diagnostic report now includes a `metricValidation` table for the overall maturity score and each layer-level maturity metric.

Methodology validation gates include `sap_agent_eval_tutorial_live_drift_evidence` for SAP-style tutorial live-drift rows, `agent_eval_observability_live_drift_evidence` for vladfeigin/llm-agents-evaluation-style observability rows, `hedrarag_artifact_eval_live_drift_evidence` for HedraRAG-style artifact-eval live-drift rows, `agent_eval_harness_live_drift_evidence` for agent-eval-harness rows, `strands_benchmark_harness_live_drift_evidence` for Strands benchmark-harness rows, `costnav_physical_navigation_replay_evidence` for CostNav-style physical-navigation replay rows, and `terminalworld_replay_evidence` for TerminalWorld-style terminal-task replay rows. The observability gate uses `liveDrift.liveDistribution.agentEvalObservabilityEvidenceCoverage0to1` and requires complete source/repository/license, agent config, eval dataset, prompt variant, model config, RAG index, metric config, baseline/live eval result, OpenTelemetry, Application Insights, Event Hub, Kusto policy, Fabric dashboard, alert receipt, metric-set and telemetry taxonomy, coverage, signed-evidence, and row-hash proof before external use. The HedraRAG gate uses `liveDrift.liveDistribution.hedraRagEvidenceCoverage0to1` and requires source/repository snapshot, declared-license proof or absent/unknown license review proof, paper/README refs, dataset/corpus/index/dependency/environment/run/result/plot/resource/GPU proof, workflow/framework/runtime taxonomy, latency, throughput, memory, replay pass, signed evidence, and row-hash proof before external use. The agent-eval-harness gate uses `liveDrift.liveDistribution.agentEvalHarnessTraceCoverage0to1` and `liveDrift.liveDistribution.agentEvalHarnessEvidenceCoverage0to1`, and requires source/repository/license, trace schema/collector/writer, adapter, framework, trace-mode, metric-context, dataset, task, tool schema, hallucination/pricing/metrics config, baseline/live result, comparison report, dashboard snapshot, local-storage policy, alert policy, reproducibility command, signed-evidence, and row-hash proof before external use. The Strands benchmark-harness gate uses `liveDrift.liveDistribution.strandsBenchmarkHarnessTrajectoryCoverage0to1` and `liveDrift.liveDistribution.strandsBenchmarkHarnessEvidenceCoverage0to1`, and requires source/repository/license, agent package, harness config, model route, prompt template, benchmark suite, runtime, task family, task manifest, dataset snapshot, Docker image, environment setup, tool policy, trajectory, patch artifact, test report, result and upload manifests, safety isolation, baseline/live run, alert policy, task-success, patch-apply, test-pass, latency, cost, signed-evidence, and row-hash proof before external use. The CostNav replay gate requires complete source/repository/license, benchmark spec, scenario manifest, route graph, economic-cost model, physical-agent config, simulator config, trajectory, result, metrics, replay command, CI receipt, route-type coverage, deterministic seed, scenario count, economic-cost delta, navigation success, replay pass rate, score delta, thresholds, signed evidence, and row-hash proof before external use. The TerminalWorld replay gate requires complete source/repository/paper/dataset/license proof, public recording and metadata manifests, privacy/quality filters, synthesized task proof, Docker environment proof, pre/post snapshots, state tests, AllPassing/Nop/Partial trials, agent trace, result/replay/CI proof, verified-subset human verification when claimed, counts, deterministic seed, trial metrics, replay pass rate, score delta, thresholds, signed evidence, and row-hash proof before external use.

The `rail_score_live_drift_evidence` gate uses `railScoreLiveDrift.liveDistribution.evidenceCoverage0to1` and requires source/repository/MIT-license, GitHub release, PyPI package/wheel/sdist, README, pyproject, requirements, CI/publish workflows, client/model/policy/session/middleware, telemetry, compliance, agent, integration, baseline/live result, drift statistic, alert receipt, dimension/mode/framework/provider context, signed-evidence, and row-hash proof before RAIL Score-style live-drift claims are used as external proof.

The `garage_rag_grounding_live_drift_evidence` gate uses `garageLiveDrift.liveDistribution.evidenceCoverage0to1` and requires source/repository/license, README, benchmark dataset, AMC-owned dataset manifest, paper reference, grounding annotation schema, retrieval corpus snapshot, prompt/evaluator config, baseline/live result, drift statistic, alert receipt, question/source/complexity context, grounding precision/recall, citation support, deflection, answer faithfulness, validation coverage, signed-evidence, and row-hash proof before GaRAGe-style RAG grounding live-drift claims are used as external proof.

The `llm_prompting_tests_public_methodology_evidence` gate uses `methodologyVersioning.receiptHash` and `replayBenchmark.manifest.rows[].llmPromptingTestsPublicMethodology`; it requires source/repository snapshot, no-license boundary, default branch, HEAD commit, tree, README, prompt catalog tree, prompt-file refs, prompt taxonomy, test manifest, task/risk taxonomy, expected-output rubric, self-check policy, no-external-assets policy, language boundary, model/provider pool, judge calibration, run config, deterministic seed, baseline/candidate results, regression thresholds, methodology changelog, deprecation notice, migration guidance, no-prompt-copy proof, signed evidence, and row hashes before llm-prompting-tests-style public-methodology claims are used as external proof.

The `agent_defense_bench_provider_drift_evidence` gate uses `providerDriftEvalPack.rows[].agentDefenseBench`; it requires source/repository/license/default-branch, README, CHECKSUMS, CITATION, requirements, MCP server manifest, attack-bank and academic/safety/cybersecurity benchmark-suite hashes, MCP-specific suite, defense server, defense policy, run config, provider route, baseline/candidate canary results, drift statistic, alert or waiver receipt, replay command, CI receipt, MCP server count, attack suite ids, defense coverage, prompt-injection block rate, jailbreak block rate, tool-poisoning block rate, benign pass rate, thresholds, signed evidence, and row hashes before AgentDefense-Bench-style provider-drift claims are used as external proof.

The `evidra_provider_drift_evidence` gate uses `providerDriftEvalPack.rows[].evidraMissingReasons`; it requires source/repository/Apache-2.0-license/default-branch/release proof, README, go.mod, CI and release workflows, Docker, CLI tree, MCP tree, API command, evidence signer and evidence packages, evlock, execcontract, export, MCP server, proxy, lifecycle service, pipeline bridge, score compare, tests/docs/signal-validation refs, prescribe/report/record/validate/scorecard command hashes, prescribe/report protocol proof, provider route, baseline/candidate canary results, baseline/live sample manifests, drift statistic, alert or waiver receipt, replay command, CI receipt, no-source-copy proof, signed evidence-chain proof, signed evidence, and row hashes before Evidra-style provider-drift claims are used as external proof.

The `ravig_bench_metric_validity_evidence` gate uses `metricValidation.rows[].ravigBenchCoverage`; it requires source/repository/Apache-2.0-license/default-branch proof, README/legal/dependency/config refs, content/design/execution/function-scoring refs, dataset/test-case/model-result refs, visually-rich generation taxonomy, RAG retrieval context, multi-modal evaluator ids, screenshot/run-script refs, metric names, CI reporter, validation pass rate, dataset case count, visual-design check count, evaluator count, owner, sample size, confidence interval, no-source-copy proof, signed evidence, report artifact hashes, and row hashes before RAViG-Bench-style metric-validity claims are used as external proof.

The `paper_read_skill_live_drift_evidence` gate uses `paperReadSkillLiveDrift.paperReadSkillEvidenceCoverage0to1`; it requires source/repository/no-license/default-branch proof, README, llms manifest, skills tree, paper-analysis and blog-reading skills, prompt catalogs, route policy, research task manifest, evaluation rubric, baseline distribution, live sample manifest, drift statistic, alert receipt, replay command, CI receipt, no-prompt-copy proof, route/task ids, paper corpus, prompt route, response, evaluator trace, signed evidence, and row hashes before paper-read-skill-style live-drift claims are used as external proof.

The `eval_ai_library_question_explainability_evidence` gate uses `questionExplainability.rows[].evalAiLibraryQuestionLens[]`; it requires source/repository/Apache-2.0-license/default-branch proof, README, LICENSE, NOTICE, pyproject, requirements, eval_lib tree, metric, agent-metric, security-metric, tracing, schema, dashboard, eval-pack, dataset, question set, question trace, evaluator config, metric result, score breakdown, rejected-evidence ledger, repair hint, regression threshold, CI receipt, no-source-copy proof, signed evidence, and row hashes before eval-ai-library-style question-explainability claims are used as external proof.

The `fore_public_methodology_versioning_evidence` gate uses `methodologyVersioning.receiptHash` and `forePublicMethodologyVersioning`; it requires methodology id/version/hash, changelog, deprecation notice, migration guidance, source/repository snapshot, archived-state proof, Apache-2.0 license, default branch, HEAD commit, tree, README, LICENSE, pyproject, package version, fore and foresight trees, api_v1.yaml, client, schema, client_test, build-test-lint workflow, eval-pack, dataset, evaluator config, baseline/candidate results, regression threshold, CI receipt, no-source-copy proof, signed evidence, and row hashes before fore-style public methodology versioning claims are used as external proof.

The `heurekabench_scientific_replay_evidence` gate uses `replayBenchmark.manifest.rows[].heurekaBenchScientificReplay`; it requires source/repository snapshot, no-root-license boundary, default branch, HEAD commit, tree, README, project/arXiv refs, benchmark JSONs, single-cell dataset manifest and checksum refs, dataset no-copy proof, benchmark validation tree, paper/PDF manifest, insight/question/answer manifests, extraction and evaluation scripts, G-Eval prompt refs, baseline runner refs, Biomni/CellVoyager adapter refs, result manifest, replay command, CI receipt, question/tool-use/domain coverage, deterministic seed, evaluator agreement, replay pass rate, score delta, thresholds, signed evidence, and row hashes before HeurekaBench-style scientific replay claims are used as external proof.

The `rag_contradiction_detector_replay_evidence` gate uses `replayBenchmark.manifest.rows[].ragContradictionDetectorReplay`; it requires source/repository snapshot, no-root-license boundary, default branch, HEAD commit, tree, README, requirements, Makefile, CI workflow, app/source/evaluation trees, SciFact fixture manifest, eval reports, quality-gate report, heuristic and torch verifier refs, PubMed ingestion, retrieval and vector-store proof, Docker/k8s/Prometheus refs, replay command, deterministic seed, baseline/candidate results, retrieval and verdict metrics, thresholds, no-source-copy proof, no-PubMed-abstract-copy proof, signed evidence, and row hashes before RAG_Contradiction_Detector-style replay claims are used as external proof.

The `skillmatch_resume_live_drift_evidence` gate uses `skillMatchResumeLiveDrift.skillMatchEvidenceCoverage0to1`; it requires source/repository snapshot, no-license boundary, default branch, HEAD commit, tree, README, Dockerfile, frontend and old-version refs, analyzer and PDF extractor refs, provider-route proof, resume task taxonomy, RAG input corpus, baseline/live sample manifests, drift statistic, alert receipt, replay command, CI receipt, privacy boundary, no-source-copy proof, no-resume-copy proof, signed evidence, and row hashes before SkillMatch-style live-drift claims are used as external proof.

The `decibench_voice_live_drift_evidence` gate uses `decibenchVoiceLiveDrift.decibenchEvidenceCoverage0to1`; it requires source/repository snapshot, license and GitHub NOASSERTION boundary, default branch, HEAD commit, tree, release, README, pyproject, CI, CLI, MCP, RAG, evaluator, audio, scenario, bridge, dashboard, docs, deterministic/semantic/RAG evaluation manifests, baseline/live sample manifests, drift statistic, alert receipt, replay command, CI receipt, privacy boundary, no-source-copy proof, no-transcript-copy proof, signed evidence, and row hashes before Decibench-style voice live-drift claims are used as external proof.

The `rag_eval_dataset_replay_evidence` gate uses `replayBenchmark.manifest.rows[].ragEvaluation.ragEvalDataset*` and requires complete source/repository/license, input-document, processor, prompt, generator, QA-dataset, endpoint config, endpoint response trace, ranking report, evaluation run, replay command, CI receipt, data formats, endpoint modes, question and endpoint counts, deterministic seed, score delta, replay pass, endpoint response coverage, thresholds, signed evidence, and row-hash proof before rag-eval-style document QA replay claims are used as external proof.

The `encourage_rag_replay_evidence` gate uses `replayBenchmark.manifest.rows[].ragEvaluation.encourage*` and requires complete source/repository/license, package/dependency, RAG method, inference-runner, template, vector DB, dataset, query, reference-answer, metric-suite, MLflow-run, result, replay-command, CI-receipt, method/backend/vector-DB/metric ids, document/question counts, deterministic seed, score-delta, replay-pass, metric-coverage, threshold, signed-evidence, and row-hash proof before Encourage-style modular RAG replay claims are used as external proof.

The `effect_autoagent_replay_evidence` gate uses `effectAutoAgentReplay.manifest.rows[]` and requires complete source/repository/MIT-license/default-branch, README, package manifest, lockfile, CI workflow, benchmark runner, harness spec, task spec, metrics, experiment log, agent blueprint, runner, run result, trajectory converter, container manager, task manifest, instruction, fixture test, Docker environment, replay command, fixed seed, baseline/candidate results, score delta, replay pass rate, CI receipt, signed evidence, and row-hash proof before effect-autoagent-style replay claims are used as external proof.

TerminalWorld-style terminal-task replay claims require benchmark id/version, source/repository/paper/dataset/license refs, public recording and metadata manifests, privacy and quality filter reports, synthesized task instruction, reference solution, task metadata, Dockerfile and Docker image proof, environment reproduction log, pre/post execution snapshots, state-test suite and result hashes, AllPassing/Nop/Partial trial proof, agent run trace, result manifest, replay command, CI receipt, verified-subset human verification when claimed, task/category/unique-command/reproduced-environment counts, deterministic seed, trial pass/failure metrics, state-assertion coverage, replay pass rate, score delta, thresholds, signed evidence refs, and row hashes; a TerminalWorld label, repository metadata, dataset card, copied README table, task count, local Docker run, aggregate pass rate, command count, or model/provider label alone is not enough.

The table records:

- metric id
- metric owner
- sample size
- construct-validity score
- counterfactual responsiveness when pre-registered intervention checks are supplied
- validation facet coverage when capability-facet checks are supplied
- confounder-control coverage when framework, scaffold, tool, or environment controls are supplied
- target outcome alignment when outcome checks are supplied
- process evidence coverage when trajectory defect or control-preservation checks are supplied
- safety-utility coverage when untrusted-tool safety and utility checks are supplied
- modality-transformation coverage when benchmark instances are transformed across modalities such as text to voice or audio variants
- lifecycle-observability coverage when metrics depend on runtime evaluator, validation, trace, lifecycle-state, or production-monitoring evidence
- ranking-stability coverage when checkpoint, model, or candidate ranking metrics depend on subsampling confidence, tail-failure, data-quality, or OCR/readability evidence
- tool-sandbox coverage when dynamic, interdependent, stateful tool environments depend on tool registries, dependency graphs, seeded state, API-failure schedules, retrieval traces, environment verification, trajectories, or recovery evidence
- continual-learning coverage when lifelong or continual-learning metrics depend on task sequences, dataset versions, retention, adaptation, forgetting-rate, environment/config, controller-log, or longitudinal-run evidence; typed game-agent claims can additionally bind game build, mod manifest, LLM config, prompt language, memory artifact, conversation log, run summary JSON, gameplay log, decision trace, outcome metrics, improvement trend, fallback-mode controls, run count, and confidence interval proof
- strategic-interaction coverage when multi-agent strategic metrics depend on player rosters, public transcripts, private actions, collision/rule audits, scoring/rating evidence, silent baselines, truncation/context controls, or pairwise uncertainty evidence
- architecture-reality coverage when agent architecture metrics depend on wrapper-agent, marketing-agent, and real-agent baselines, planning hierarchy, memory/context retention, recovery strategy, stress-tool-failure, network-resilience, cost-per-success, ensemble-coordination, and statistical-confidence evidence
- RAG-pipeline coverage when retrieval-augmented generation metrics depend on custom document sets, test sets, domain/jurisdiction/language/task coverage, corpus/chunking, index provenance, solution rosters/configs, retriever/reranker/model/judge configs, selected metric lists, query-level results, metric-computation traces, logged samples, retrieval traces, generation traces, evaluator configuration, report/export artifacts, or performance/cost evidence
- RAG evaluation-pipeline coverage when `requireRagEvaluationPipelineProof` is set for metrics that claim to evaluate a RAG pipeline against ground-truth question/answer data; each row must bind signed evidence for ground-truth questions, ground-truth answers, RAG pipeline config, document corpus, metric definitions, query results, retrieval traces, generation traces, evaluator config, evaluation report, metric owner, sample size, and confidence interval
- MIRAGE-style RAG metric coverage when `requireMirageRagMetricProof` is set for metric-intensive RAG validity claims; each row must bind signed evidence for benchmark identity, dataset manifest, QA-pair manifest, context-pool manifest, retrieval-pool manifest, base/oracle/mixed protocol proof, retriever config, model config, LLM result report, retriever result report, MIRAGE metric report, overall-score formula, metric owner, sample size, and confidence interval
- ARIASHA/MiRAGE-style drug-repositioning coverage when `requireMirageDrugRepositioningProof` is set for drug-disease association and drug-repositioning metric-validity claims; each row must bind signed evidence for benchmark identity, dataset release, train/test split, drug-disease mapping, drug feature, disease feature, similarity matrix, negative-sampling protocol, classifier config, feature-selection report, score-calculation manifest, evaluation report, case-study validation, metric owner, sample size, and confidence interval
- Legal Code RAG metric coverage when `requireLegalCodeRagProof` is set for French legal-code RAG validity claims; each row must bind signed evidence for legal corpus manifests, Legifrance source boundary, retriever config, vector database config, embedding model config, windowing, hybrid-search, query-rewrite, routing-policy configs, evaluation dataset, reference answers, metric definitions, evaluator config, evaluation report, metric owner, sample size, and confidence interval
- GuardBench-style guardrail metric coverage when `requireGuardbenchMetricProof` is set for guardrail-model and prompt-moderation metric-validity claims; each row must bind signed evidence for benchmark identity, dataset manifest, dataset access policy, standardized-format proof, moderation-function contract, guardrail-model config, threshold config, prediction-score manifest, metric-suite report, confusion-matrix report, language coverage, leaderboard/export report, metric owner, sample size, and confidence interval
- business-workflow coverage when workflow-automation metrics depend on domain/task coverage, simple-baseline checks, public/private score caveats, toolset/config controls, programmatic end-state assertions, partial-credit and strict pass-rate separation, export artifacts, or multi-run comparison evidence
- data-agent analytical coverage when heterogeneous data-agent benchmark metrics depend on task-type coverage, database/source-modality coverage, difficulty distribution, metric-computation traces, agent-workflow roster/config evidence, expert-validation evidence, cost/latency traces, and submission-schema evidence
- embodied-agent coverage when simulator benchmark metrics depend on task-type coverage, simulator environment config, scene or dataset package, random/human/model baselines, action-observation trajectory evidence, result folder evidence, overall and task-type metric reports, metric owner, sample size, and confidence interval
- evaluator-suite coverage when `requireEvaluatorSuiteProof` is set for metrics that use deterministic assertions, LLM judges, safety assertions, red-team attacks, dataset eval manifests, custom judges, reporter outputs, framework integrations, threshold configs, metric owners, sample sizes, and confidence intervals as external validity proof
- pentest benchmark coverage when `requirePentestBenchmarkProof` is set for metrics that use pentesting, exploit-development, smart-contract exploitation, or threat-model benchmark suites; each row must bind signed evidence for source/repository/license proof, benchmark release manifests, task-id manifests, target-image manifests, runtime-controller manifests, firewall-isolation configs, LLM-proxy configs, smart-contract dataset manifests, historical fork manifests, problem metadata, FlawVerifier contract manifests, Forge grader results, profit-threshold metrics, anti-cheat reset proof, dataset cutoff splits, Dockerized app manifests, language-stack coverage, vulnerability-class coverage, difficulty distribution, multi-step chain coverage, flag ground truth, threat-model ground truth, false-positive traps, security-control effectiveness, exploit execution traces, exploit-success metrics, threat-model reports, metric owner, sample size, and confidence interval
- trace-evaluation coverage when `requireTraceEvaluationProof` is set for metrics that use trace-derived agent evaluation; each row must bind signed evidence for Bedrock Converse-style model config, agent-parameter manifest, tool registry, trace manifest, repeatable case manifest, dynamic expectation validator, bulk case run manifest, run permutation manifest, mock LLM backend control, metric definition manifest, measurement export manifest, production monitor binding, threshold alarm config, metric owner, sample size, and confidence interval
- living-environment coverage when `requireLivingEnvironmentProof` is set for metrics that use Terrarium-style stateful multi-turn environments; each row must bind signed evidence for task programs, mutable environment manifests, environment-mutation traces, capability manifests, sandbox providers, agent adapters, multi-turn trajectories, stage checkers, checker results, trial results, aggregate metrics, pass@k metrics, proactive triggers, metric owner, sample size, and confidence interval
- mobile-agent coverage when `requireMobileAgentProof` is set for metrics that use MobileBench-style mobile-agent evaluation; each row must bind signed evidence for benchmark/source refs, mobile environment manifests, app inventory, API catalogs, UI automation traces, task datasets, task-complexity groups, multi-app task manifests, checkpoint rubrics and results, reset policies, device-state fixtures, result reports, dataset license boundaries, metric owner, sample size, and confidence interval
- geospatial provider-drift evidence when a provider canary uses GeoBenchX-style geospatial tool-calling; each row must bind signed evidence for geospatial benchmark ids, task-set hashes, dataset snapshots, tool registries, reference solutions, trace exports, judge panels/configs, human calibration, result reports, token-cost reports, complexity groups, solvable and unsolvable task counts, tool counts, max iterations, and row hashes
- scenario-simulation replay evidence when a replay-corpus row uses human/LLM co-participation, action-level evaluation, web visualization, persistence, or checkpoint resume; each row must bind signed evidence for scenario project, scene and role definitions, participant policy, agent roster, evaluator and action schema, task dataset, web UI, server/container/persistence/checkpoint artifacts, event and action traces, evaluation report, visualization artifact, replay command, deterministic seed, action metrics, and row hashes
- warehouse-native LLM eval replay evidence when a replay-corpus row uses dbt evaluation, warehouse AI functions, LLM-as-judge criteria, baseline versioning, drift detection, or no-data-egress claims; each row must bind signed evidence for dbt project/package manifests, warehouse adapter and AI-function manifests, capture schema, baseline versions, judge criteria, sampling/threshold configs, raw/evaluation/score/performance/drift/alert artifacts, compiled SQL, run result, no-egress policy, replay command, deterministic seed, metrics, and row hashes
- edge AI agent replay evidence when a replay-corpus row uses edge/on-device multimodal-agent, mobile/embedded/wearable/IoT device, client runtime, inference-engine, optimization, offline, privacy, latency, memory, energy, accuracy, replay pass-rate, or score-delta claims; each row must bind signed evidence for source/repository/license, device profile, runtime manifest, optimization manifest, benchmark dataset, task manifest, application scenario, replay command, metrics report, device-class/modality/runtime-kind coverage, on-device/offline/privacy flags, metric thresholds, and row hashes
- MedAsk clinical benchmark replay evidence when a replay-corpus row uses SymptomCheck diagnostic accuracy, Triage urgency classification, OSCE-style symptom assessment, or clinical-vignette replay claims; each row must bind signed evidence for repository/source/license, requirements, setup, SymptomCheck and Triage vignette manifests, evaluation scripts, patient simulator, doctor and triage model configs, result manifests, paired analysis, run and replay commands, deterministic seed, diagnostic and triage metrics, thresholds, and row hashes

- Realign simulation coverage when `requireRealignSimulationProof` is set for metrics that use simulation-driven AI app testing; each row must bind signed evidence for source/repository/license refs, YAML config manifests, app-under-test manifests, dataset manifests, scenario manifests, synthetic-user persona manifests, evaluator registries, evaluator targets, simulation run traces, repeated-run traces, judge calibration reports, statistical rigor reports, CI regression manifests, experiment tracking manifests, result artifacts, metric owner, sample size, and confidence interval
- HumanStudy-Bench coverage when `requireHumanStudyBenchProof` is set for metrics that use participant simulation, human-study response comparison, social-science simulation, evaluator/scorer validity, reliability reports, or validation-pipeline proof; each row must bind signed evidence for source/repository/license refs, default-branch snapshots, study configs, participant backgrounds, human responses, agent responses, evaluator registries, metric definitions, validators, scorers, standardizers, inter-rater agreement, test-retest reliability, validation pipelines, result artifacts, CI reporters, metric owner, sample size, and confidence interval
- RAGAS notebook metric-validity coverage when `requireRagasNotebookProof` is set for metrics that use Coding-Crashkurse/RAG-Evaluation-with-Ragas-style RAGAS notebooks; each row must bind signed evidence for source/no-license-boundary refs, notebook manifests, dependency manifests, document corpus, chunking, testset generator, evolution mix, generated testset, RAG chain, retriever/vectorstore, model/embedding, answer-context trace, RAGAS metric suite/result, LangFuse score export, visualization artifact, metric owner, sample size, and confidence interval
- inter-rater or judge-agreement signal
- test-retest stability when prior comparable runs exist
- 95% confidence interval
- fail-closed status and warnings

Typed continual-game learning checks are for TokenSpire2-style agents that claim cross-run improvement from gameplay and persistent memory. When a `continualSignalType` is supplied, AMC evaluates required signal coverage across task sequence, environment config, controller log, longitudinal run trace, game build, mod manifest, LLM config, prompt language, memory artifact, conversation log, run summary JSON, gameplay log, decision trace, run outcome metrics, improvement trend, fallback-mode control, sample size, and confidence interval evidence. Missing required signals, insufficient run count, or absent signed evidence fails closed before a point-in-time game result can support a continual-learning validity claim.

Pentest benchmark checks are for Apex-style vulnerable-app suites, ExploitGym-style exploit-development suites, SconeBench-style smart-contract exploit suites, and threat-model suites where a metric could otherwise overclaim from a final solve rate, exploit trace, profit result, or generated report. When `requirePentestBenchmarkProof` is set, AMC requires typed proof for source/repository/license refs, benchmark releases, task ids, target images, runtime controllers, firewall isolation, LLM proxy configs, smart-contract dataset manifests, historical fork manifests, problem metadata, FlawVerifier contract manifests, Forge grader results, profit-threshold metrics, anti-cheat reset proof, cutoff split manifests, Dockerized app manifests, stack/vulnerability/difficulty coverage, multi-step chains, flag and threat-model ground truth, false-positive traps, security-control effectiveness, execution traces, exploit-success metrics, structured threat-model reports, ownership, sample size, confidence interval, signed evidence refs, and row hashes. Missing required signals, invalid artifact hashes, missing suite ids, missing owner, or unusable confidence intervals fail closed before a pentesting, exploit-development, smart-contract exploitation, or threat-model benchmark metric can be used as external proof. AMC treats sunblaze-ucb/exploitgym and anthropics/scone-bench as high-level metric-validity signals only; AMC does not copy code, commands, configs, setup scripts, task data, Docker images, exploit traces, flags, vulnerability rows, smart-contract datasets, problem metadata, FlawVerifier examples, grading scripts, README prose, docs prose, diagrams, screenshots, lockfiles, third-party code, or implementation details.

GuardBench-style guardrail metric-validity claims require benchmark identity, dataset manifest, dataset access policy, standardized-format proof, moderation-function contract, guardrail-model config, threshold config, prediction-score manifest, metric-suite report, confusion-matrix report, language coverage, leaderboard/export report, metric owner, sample size, confidence interval, signed evidence refs, and row hashes. A leaderboard row, final F1 score, model label, local command log, README example, copied dataset row, or export table alone is not enough.

The same `metricValidation` object also includes:

- `evalPack`: deterministic metric-validation rows with row hashes, dataset hash, source refs, and signed evidence refs for every metric row that can be replayed.
- `ciGate`: a CI/lifecycle gate with failed metric IDs, attention metric IDs, pass/fail state, and fail-closed summary.

Default validation thresholds:

| Gate | Default |
|------|---------|
| Minimum sample size | 5 scored question samples |
| Minimum construct validity | 0.55 |
| Minimum counterfactual responsiveness | 0.60 |
| Minimum validation facet coverage | 0.75 |
| Minimum confounder control coverage | 0.75 |
| Minimum target outcome alignment | 0.75 |
| Minimum process evidence coverage | 0.75 |
| Minimum safety-utility coverage | 0.75 |
| Minimum modality-transformation coverage | 0.75 |

## Judge Calibration Receipts

AMC treats LLM-as-a-judge outputs as evaluator signals that must be calibrated before they can support external maturity claims. Judge calibration receipts are AMC-owned artifacts; they bind the judging rubric, calibration set, judge outputs, signed evidence, disagreement metrics, optional stability-aware ranking checks, optional source-specific benchmark proof, appeal outcomes, and CI/lifecycle gate state.

Graph-eval judge calibration proof is available for cache-aware, graph-based LLM/RAG/agent evaluation pipelines. AMC uses live public-source review of `harnexa/nexa-gauge` as a high-level signal for graph topology, cache-aware execution, per-case reports, cost estimates, metric branches, and LLM-as-judge proof. AMC does not copy its code, commands, configuration, examples, prompts, sample reports, diagrams, or implementation details.

AgentStockBenchmarkResults-style judge calibration proof is available for future-outcome market-ranking claims where the judge is the later market result. AMC uses live public-source review of `xsunsim/AgentStockBenchmarkResults` as a high-level signal for S&P 500 ranking, daily PnL accounting, leaderboard export, manifests, prompts, strategies, data snapshots, and appealable score review. AMC does not copy its code, commands, configs, market data, leaderboard rows, prompts, strategy files, generated reports, images, README prose, or implementation details.

Each receipt records:

- rubric id, rubric version, criteria, owner, and rubric hash
- calibration-set id, version, row count, per-row hashes, dataset hash, optional task category, prompt artifact hash, output artifact hash, and subjective/objective expected scores
- per-judge scores with prompt hashes, output hashes, optional subjective/objective scores, optional judge memory reference, evidence refs, and signed evidence refs
- inter-judge agreement, mean absolute error against expected scores, subjective/objective calibration error, max score variance, task-category distribution, and failed item ids
- optional pointwise/listwise/pairwise stability checks with subsample counts, ranking stability, percentile scores, tail-failure rate, data quality, OCR readability, checkpoint id, and signed evidence
- optional graph-eval proof with graph id/version, node graph hash, scan node hash, metric-node hashes, aggregation node hash, report artifact hash, cache-key hash, model-routing hash, prompt/parser/cost/report/schema/dataset/execution hashes, required and executed metric branches, metric branch coverage, per-case report coverage, cache hit rate, estimated and actual cost, cost-estimate drift, signed evidence refs, and graph proof hash
- optional AgentStock-style proof with source repository, commit/tree/license/README/pyproject refs, accounting/leaderboard/manifest/prompt/ranking/portfolio/strategy/data/script/digest refs, benchmark date, market universe, agent roster, prediction prompt, future-outcome window, ground-truth price data, ranking result, PnL metric, appeal workflow, replay command, CI receipt, coverage metrics, signed evidence refs, and proof hash
- appeal status and signed appeal evidence for failed rows
- source refs, aggregate signed evidence refs, replayable state, fail-closed state, and receipt hash

Default judge-calibration thresholds:

| Gate | Default |
|------|---------|
| Minimum calibration rows | 3 rows |
| Minimum judges per item | 2 judges |
| Minimum inter-judge agreement | 0.80 on a 0-1 scale |
| Maximum mean absolute error | 0.15 on a 0-1 scale |
| Maximum subjective mean absolute error | 0.15 on a 0-1 scale |
| Maximum objective mean absolute error | 0.15 on a 0-1 scale |
| Maximum score variance | 0.05 |
| Minimum stability subsample count | 5 |
| Minimum ranking stability | 0.75 |
| Maximum tail failure rate | 0.20 |
| Minimum data quality | 0.70 |
| Minimum OCR readability | 0.70 |
| Require graph proof for LLM judge | false |
| Minimum graph metric branch coverage | 1.00 |
| Minimum graph per-case report coverage | 1.00 |
| Maximum graph cost-estimate drift ratio | 0.25 |
| Minimum AgentStock ranked agent count | 2 |
| Minimum AgentStock ticker count | 20 |
| Minimum AgentStock trading day count | 5 |
| Minimum AgentStock future-outcome coverage | 1.00 |
| Minimum AgentStock leaderboard coverage | 1.00 |
| Minimum AgentStock appeal resolution coverage | 1.00 |
| Minimum AgentStock replay pass rate | 0.95 |
| Require signed evidence | true |
| Require resolved appeals for failed rows | true |

If any threshold fails, `receipt.failClosed` and `receipt.ciGate.failClosed` are set to `true`. A failed row also needs a resolved appeal (`upheld`, `overturned`, or `rejected`) before the receipt can be replayable. Missing prompt hashes, output hashes, evidence refs, signed evidence refs, invalid prompt/output artifact hashes, missing stability-check evidence, unstable ranking, weak data/OCR quality, missing appeal outcome hashes, missing required graph proof, incomplete graph proof hashes, insufficient graph metric-branch coverage, weak per-case report coverage, excessive graph cost-estimate drift, incomplete AgentStock source refs, weak future-outcome coverage, missing leaderboard proof, weak appeal-resolution coverage, or weak AgentStock replay pass rate keep the receipt from becoming external proof.

Where users see it:

- API Score: `POST /api/v1/score/judge-calibration` returns `receipt`, `disagreement`, `ciGate`, and `failClosed`.
- API Watch: `POST /api/v1/watch/judge-calibration` returns Watch alerts for failed calibration gates, including AgentStock source, future-outcome, leaderboard, appeal, and signed-evidence failures.
- API Shield: `POST /api/v1/shield/judge-calibration/verify` recomputes the receipt hash and optional proof hashes before accepting the claim.

## Question-Level Score Explainability

Each diagnostic report includes a `questionExplainability` receipt pack. This pack makes each L0-L5 question decision replayable enough for operator review instead of only showing a final level.

For every question row AMC records:

- `questionId`, title, and consuming surfaces (`Score`, `Shield`, `Watch`)
- claimed, supported, and final maturity levels
- status: `passed`, `needs_evidence`, `capped`, or `unsupported_claim`
- evidence window summary with event count, distinct sessions, first/last timestamp, and duration
- accepted evidence IDs and signed ledger references (`eventHash`, `writerSig`, trust tier, session, timestamp)
- rejected evidence IDs with explicit rejection reasons
- component diagnostics with component id, component type, accepted/rejected/missing status, linked evidence refs, rejected evidence refs, and component-specific repair hint
- evaluation criteria diagnostics with criterion id, criterion type (`policy_gate`, `unit_test`, `shell_interaction`, `file_comparison`, `agent_judge`, `human_review`, `long_horizon_state`, `memory_continuity`, `delayed_outcome`, `adversarial_detection`, `prompt_artifact_alignment`, `subjective_quality`, `objective_quality`, `task_category_coverage`, `temporal_forecast_horizon`, `multi_source_integration`, `tool_use_trace`, `multi_agent_orchestration`, `session_state_trace`, `tool_auth_boundary`, `code_execution_sandbox`, `untrusted_tool_feedback`, `trajectory_trust_formation`, `hidden_trigger_detection`, `final_action_risk`, `safe_control_comparison`, `router_visible_prefix`, `step_level_model_choice`, `trajectory_membership`, `downstream_success_preservation`, `cost_accounting_trace`, `redteam_challenge_scope`, `sandboxed_execution_environment`, `exploit_attempt_trace`, `flag_submission_outcome`, `step_budget_termination`, `off_policy_evaluation_protocol`, `logged_dataset_trace`, `baseline_comparison`, `code_modification_trace`, `optimization_cycle_trace`, `reliability_improvement_measure`, or `custom`), status, judge reference, linked evidence refs, and criterion-specific repair hint
- rubric lens diagnostics with rubric id/version/source, skill type, score, grade, Deep Review certificate hash, market-signal refs, check ids, pillars, pass/partial/fail/not-applicable status, weights, accepted evidence refs, rejected evidence refs, and check-specific fix hints
- Promptflow-style RAG flow diagnostics with flow id, vector-search backend, flow DAG hash, parameter config hash, eval set hash, batch run id, evaluator flow hash, ground-truth column, data mapping hash, variant/config hashes, deployment artifact hash, metric ids, status, accepted/rejected evidence refs, and repair hint
- AI-coding landscape lens diagnostics with landscape id, source ref, category, dataset refs, dataset SHA-256 hashes, update cadence, verification freshness, cohort refs, benchmark or tool/model refs, status, accepted/rejected evidence refs, and repair hint for fast-moving coding-agent, CLI, IDE, app-builder, OSS tooling, survey, model, and leaderboard claims
- benchmark-submission lens diagnostics with benchmark id, source ref, submission id/version, agent version, submission timestamp, task id/category/status, grading type, overall and category scores, speed and cost fields, leaderboard metric views, submission metadata hash, task-breakdown hash, leaderboard snapshot hash, criterion-level score/status/grading/evidence rows, accepted/rejected evidence refs, repair hint, and row hash for task-level benchmark submission claims
- test-suite evaluation lens diagnostics with suite id, source ref, language, test framework, adapter, dataset ref/hash, test-case id/hash, evaluator ids, evaluator-config hash, judge model ref, experiment run/result/export hashes, CI run/config proof, trace and tool-call validation hashes when agent behavior is claimed, pass-rate and score thresholds, cost/latency/token metrics where claimed, status, accepted/rejected evidence refs, repair hint, and row hash
- multi-user benchmark lens diagnostics with benchmark id, source ref, scenario id/family, capability, dataset/user-role/policy/preference/queue/instruction/trace/evaluator/result/metric hashes, user-role and turn counts, scenario-specific privacy/coordination/fairness/instruction metrics and thresholds, status, accepted/rejected evidence refs, repair hint, and row hash
- continual-learning benchmark lens diagnostics with benchmark id, source ref, domain id, workflow id, dataset/state-schema/initial-state/state-mutation/conversation/entity-relationship/tool-execution/evaluator/result/replay hashes, memory-policy and adaptive-learning proof when claimed, scenario/turn/state-mutation/entity counts, task-completion, response-quality, state-accuracy, retention, token-cost thresholds where claimed, status, accepted/rejected evidence refs, repair hint, and row hash
- SRE incident-triage lens diagnostics with OpenEnv environment id, source ref, task and scenario ids, difficulty/severity, OpenEnv config hash, scenario manifest hash, incident report hash, raw log bundle hash, metric snapshot hash, user report hash, action payload hash, deterministic grader config hash, feedback hash, reward/root-cause/red-herring/ordered-remediation scores and thresholds, step bounds, status, accepted/rejected evidence refs, repair hint, and row hash
- missing gate reasons, including higher L-level gate failures and cap flags
- a repair hint tied to the question's upgrade and evidence-gate guidance
- deterministic row hash plus a report-level manifest hash

`questionExplainability.failClosed` is `true` when any question lacks accepted evidence, has missing gates, is capped, has an unsupported claim, includes a failed/missing evaluation criterion, includes a failed/missing RAG flow diagnostic, includes a stale/incomplete AI-coding landscape lens, includes an incomplete benchmark-submission lens, includes an incomplete test-suite evaluation lens, includes an incomplete multi-user benchmark lens, includes an incomplete professional-task lens, includes an incomplete IoT firmware lens, includes an incomplete retail-sales lens, includes an incomplete continual-learning benchmark lens, includes an incomplete SRE incident-triage lens, or includes a partial/failing rubric lens check. `questionExplainability.replayable` is only `true` when every row has accepted signed evidence references, replayable evaluation criteria, replayable RAG flow diagnostics, replayable AI-coding landscape lenses, replayable benchmark-submission lenses, replayable test-suite evaluation lenses, replayable multi-user benchmark lenses, replayable professional-task lenses, replayable IoT firmware lenses, replayable retail-sales lenses, replayable continual-learning benchmark lenses, replayable SRE incident-triage lenses, replayable rubric lens checks, and any supplied Deep Review, flow artifact, landscape dataset, benchmark-submission, test-suite, multi-user scenario, professional-task, IoT firmware, retail-sales, continual-learning stateful-workflow, or incident-triage proof hash is a valid SHA-256 digest. This means a report can still be useful for diagnosis while explicitly refusing to become external proof when evidence is incomplete or criterion-level/RAG-flow/landscape/benchmark-submission/test-suite/multi-user/professional-task/IoT-firmware/retail-sales/continual-learning/incident-triage/rubric-level evaluation disagrees with the aggregate question score.

AI-coding landscape or leaderboard question-explainability claims require source category, dataset refs and SHA-256 hashes, update cadence, freshness, cohort refs, benchmark or tool/model refs, accepted evidence, rejected-evidence reasons, and a repair hint. A category listing alone is not enough because coding-agent, CLI, IDE, app-builder, OSS tooling, survey, model, and leaderboard ecosystems change too quickly for stale source metadata to be used as proof.

Benchmark-submission question-explainability claims require benchmark/source identity, submission id/version, agent version, submission timestamp, task id/category/status, grading type, overall and category scores, speed and cost metrics where claimed, leaderboard metric views, submission metadata hash, task-breakdown hash, leaderboard snapshot hash, criterion-level scores, evidence refs, rejected-evidence refs, repair hint, and row hash. An aggregate submission score alone is not enough because the operator needs to see which task criterion, status indicator, or missing replay artifact moved the L0-L5 question.

Test-suite question-explainability claims require suite id, language, framework, adapter, dataset ref/hash, test-case hash, evaluator ids/config, judge model where claimed, experiment run/result/export artifacts, CI run/config, agent trace and tool-call validation when agent behavior is claimed, pass-rate and score thresholds, cost/latency/tokens where claimed, accepted evidence, rejected-evidence reasons, repair hint, and row-hash evidence. A console output, passing unit test, aggregate pass rate, or local CI log alone is not enough because the operator needs to see which dataset, evaluator, CI run, trace, and artifact moved the L0-L5 question.

Multi-user LLM-agent question-explainability claims require benchmark/source identity, scenario id/family, capability label, dataset manifest hash, user-role manifest hash, scenario-specific permission/preference/queue/instruction proof, interaction trace hash, evaluator config hash, result artifact hash, metric report hash, user-role and turn counts, scenario-specific metric thresholds, accepted evidence, rejected-evidence reasons, repair hint, and row-hash evidence. A README scenario summary, aggregate score, local run output, copied dataset path, transcript snippet, or source metadata alone is not enough because the operator needs to see which user-role, policy, trace, evaluator, and metric threshold moved the L0-L5 question.

CL-Bench-style continual-learning question-explainability claims require benchmark/source identity, domain id, workflow id, dataset manifest, state schema, initial state, state mutation trace, conversation trace, entity-relationship graph, tool-execution trace, evaluator config, result artifact, replay command, memory policy when claimed, adaptive-learning trace when claimed, scenario/turn/state-mutation/entity counts, task-completion, response-quality, state-accuracy, retention, token-cost thresholds where claimed, accepted evidence, rejected-evidence reasons, repair hint, and row-hash evidence. A repository title, CRM label, seed label, local transcript, copied task prompt, aggregate score, task-completion percentage, response-quality score, token cost, model/provider label, or source metadata alone is not enough because the operator needs to see which stateful workflow, mutation, entity, tool, evaluator, and retention gate moved the L0-L5 question.

Hermes Turbo-style performance question-explainability claims require benchmark/source identity, repository and license refs, live default-branch commit/tree, README and package manifests, benchmark/perf-budget/daily-score workflows, turbo-score script, performance dashboard, benchmark report, baseline/candidate results, latency and throughput traces, score manifest, regression thresholds, CI config/run, performance facet, run count, p50/p95 latency, throughput, speedup, dashboard coverage, regression pass rate, accepted evidence, rejected-evidence reasons, repair hint, and row-hash evidence. A repository title, default branch, star count, README performance claim, local command, dashboard screenshot, aggregate speedup, CI badge, model/provider label, or source metadata alone is not enough because the operator needs to see which measured hot path, dashboard metric, CI threshold, and regression gate moved the L0-L5 question.

RSS market-impact alert methodology claims require methodology id, methodology version, methodology hash, changelog row, deprecation notice, migration guidance, source repository snapshot, no-license boundary, feed source, polling window, model provider route, prompt/schema policy, importance taxonomy, asset-class taxonomy, dedupe ledger, analysis ledger, push policy, rate-limit policy, alert threshold, evaluator/backtest report, outcome-window policy, cost and latency accounting, accepted evidence, rejected-evidence reasons, signed evidence refs, and row hashes.
