High-risk deployments should not treat a strong question receipt as a substitute for human-interaction evaluation. The Nature Medicine randomized study on LLM medical assistants found that strong standalone model performance and standard benchmark performance did not predict failures when members of the public interacted with the systems. For AMC, this means question receipts must be paired with signed evidence from realistic user workflows before claims about public-facing or high-risk deployment reliability are accepted.

Where users see it:

- CLI/JSON: run a diagnostic with `amc run --json` or load a saved run; the JSON includes `questionExplainability`.
- Studio: open Diagnostic, then select `Open evidence drilldown` on a question card to inspect that question's source artifacts, evidence-window coverage, evaluation criteria, rubric lens previews, RAG flow diagnostics, AI-coding landscape lenses, benchmark-submission lenses, test-suite evaluation lenses, multi-user benchmark lenses, continual-learning benchmark lenses, SRE incident-triage lenses, Scorable SDK Studio drilldown rows, accepted and rejected evidence preview, missing gate reasons, and repair hint.
- Markdown: generated reports include `## Question Score Explainability`.
- API: `GET /api/v1/score/run/:runId`, `GET /api/v1/score/report/:runId`, `GET /api/v1/score/evidence-drilldown/:runId/:questionId`, `POST /api/v1/watch/explain`, and `GET /api/v1/shield/score-explainability/:runId`.
- Passport: compact passports can carry `maturity.questionExplainabilityHash` so downstream proof bundles can bind to the detailed receipt without exposing all question evidence.

## Provider and Model Drift Canary Checks

Provider and model changes must be tested with explicit canary rows before their results are treated as comparable to an earlier diagnostic or benchmark run.

`amc benchmark provider-drift --file <json>` compares baseline and candidate rows by `provider`, `model`, and `canaryId`. Each row records:

- provider, model, and version
- optional benchmark family and capability id for targeted canaries
- optional arena, environment, and reference-pool identifiers for multi-agent or tournament canaries
- optional evaluator framework id/version, provider route, metric suite id, metric ids/count, evaluator config hash, generated test-data hash, verdict aggregation mode/config hash, verdict temperature/power-mean parameter when applicable, and dashboard artifact hash for Eval-ai-library-style evaluator framework canaries
- optional Falcon Evaluate proof for provider-drift canaries: source/repository/license/default-branch hashes, release tag, package/lockfile/requirements hashes, README/docs/workflow hashes, evaluation/context/fairness/reliability/security/ethics/results/plot/user-analytics module hashes, validation data schema hash, metric-family ids, metric ids/count, provider route id, and baseline/candidate canary-result hashes
- optional observability pipeline proof for Opik/ZenML/Mongo-style canaries: pipeline orchestrator id, pipeline run id, experiment tracker id, experiment run id, observability project id, datastore id, retrieval index hash, content dataset hash, summary artifact hash, QA dataset hash, trace export hash, metric report hash, and pipeline config hash
- optional geospatial tool-calling proof for GeoBenchX-style canaries: geospatial benchmark id, task-set hash, dataset snapshot hash, tool-registry hash, reference-solution hash, trace export hash, judge panel id, judge config hash, human-calibration hash, result-report hash, token-cost report hash, task-complexity groups, solvable/unsolvable task counts, tool count, and max tool iterations
- optional AgentDefense-Bench proof for MCP security-defense canaries: source/repository/license/default-branch hashes, README/CHECKSUMS/CITATION/requirements hashes, MCP server manifest hash, attack-bank and benchmark-suite hashes, MCP-specific suite hash, defense server hash, defense policy hash, run config hash, provider route id, canary result hashes, drift statistic hash, alert/waiver hash, replay command hash, CI receipt hash, MCP server count, attack suite ids, defense coverage, prompt-injection block rate, jailbreak block rate, tool-poisoning block rate, and benign pass rate
- optional Evidra proof for DevOps MCP provider-drift canaries: source/repository/license/default-branch hashes, release tag, README/go.mod/workflow/Docker/CLI/MCP/API hashes, evidence-chain package hashes, lifecycle/pipeline/score/test/docs refs, prescribe/report/record/validate/scorecard command hashes, prescribe/report protocol proof, provider route id, canary result hashes, baseline/live sample manifest hashes, drift statistic hash, alert/waiver hash, replay command hash, CI receipt hash, no-source-copy proof hash, and signed evidence-chain hash
- canary sample size
- optional trajectory count
- quality score mean
- refusal-rate mean
- optional invalid-action rate
- optional error-attribution rate
- optional judge-agreement rate
- optional unjudged-prediction rate
- optional architectural repair-effectiveness score
- optional false-positive identification score
- optional net codebase impact score
- optional protocol success rate
- optional agreement or deal rate
- optional target outcome value
- optional latent-preference alignment score
- optional user-aware progress AUC and progress-per-turn scores
- optional pass@k and pass^k reliability scores
- optional subgoal completion and expected-tool-call coverage scores
- optional persona coverage across expert, non-expert, or other user-proxy cohorts
- optional clustered error rate from automated error analysis
- p95 latency
- mean cost
- trace, dataset, approval, or benchmark evidence references
- signed evidence references when the run is used as a replayable eval pack

Default fail-closed thresholds are:

| Gate | Default |
|------|---------|
| Minimum sample size | 5 canary samples |
| Minimum trajectory count | 0 trajectories |
| Maximum score drop | 0.08 on a 0-1 score scale |
| Maximum refusal-rate increase | 0.08 |
| Maximum invalid-action rate increase | 0.05 |
| Maximum error-attribution rate increase | 0.05 |
| Maximum judge-agreement drop | 0.08 |
| Maximum unjudged-prediction rate increase | 0.10 |
| Maximum architectural repair-effectiveness drop | 0.10 |
| Maximum false-positive identification drop | 0.10 |
| Maximum net codebase impact drop | 0.10 |
| Maximum artifact accuracy drop | 0.10 |
| Maximum formula integrity drop | 0.10 |
| Maximum artifact format quality drop | 0.10 |
| Maximum protocol success rate drop | 0.08 |
| Maximum agreement or deal rate drop | 0.08 |
| Maximum target outcome value drop | 0.10 |
| Maximum latent-preference alignment drop | 0.10 |
| Maximum evaluator coverage drop | 0.10 |
| Maximum guardrail pass-rate drop | 0.08 |
| Maximum score-threshold pass-rate drop | 0.08 |
| Maximum retry-stability drop | 0.08 |
| Maximum progress AUC drop | 0.08 |
| Maximum progress per turn drop | 0.08 |
| Maximum pass@k drop | 0.08 |
| Maximum pass^k drop | 0.08 |
| Maximum subgoal completion drop | 0.08 |
| Maximum expected-tool-call coverage drop | 0.08 |
| Maximum persona coverage drop | 0.10 |
| Maximum clustered error-rate increase | 0.05 |
| Minimum evaluator metric count | 1 metric |
| Minimum AgentDefense-Bench MCP server count | 1 server |
| Minimum AgentDefense-Bench attack suite count | 2 suites |
| Minimum AgentDefense-Bench defense coverage | 0.80 |
| Maximum AgentDefense-Bench defense coverage drop | 0.08 |
| Maximum AgentDefense-Bench prompt-injection block-rate drop | 0.08 |
| Maximum AgentDefense-Bench jailbreak block-rate drop | 0.08 |
| Maximum AgentDefense-Bench tool-poisoning block-rate drop | 0.08 |
| Maximum AgentDefense-Bench benign-pass-rate drop | 0.08 |
| Maximum p95 latency increase ratio | 0.25 |
| Maximum mean-cost increase ratio | 0.35 |

Unwaived threshold breaches produce watch-compatible alerts and set `failClosed` to `true`. Provider drift canary rows may also include optional artifact-production dimensions for end-to-end deliverables: artifact accuracy, formula integrity, and artifact format quality. They may also include proxy/outcome split dimensions for hidden-preference or business-outcome canaries: protocol success rate, agreement or deal rate, target outcome value, and latent-preference alignment. Standard evaluator-suite canaries can add evaluator coverage, guardrail pass rate, score-threshold pass rate, and retry-stability evidence so a provider/model promotion can fail closed when evaluator libraries, pytest-style pass-rate gates, guardrails, or flaky/retry stability regress despite a flat headline score. Eval-ai-library-style evaluator framework canaries fail closed when they claim an evaluator framework but omit framework/version, provider route, metric suite, metric ids/count, evaluator config, generated test data, verdict aggregation config, temperature/power-mean parameters for temperature-controlled power-mean aggregation, or dashboard/report artifact hashes. Opik-style observability pipeline canaries fail closed when they claim experiment-tracked observability but omit pipeline orchestrator/run, experiment tracker/run, observability project, datastore, retrieval index, content dataset, summary artifact, QA dataset, trace export, metric report, or pipeline config proof. SparkOrbit-style orbit-monitor canaries fail closed with `orbitMonitorEvidence` when they claim fast-moving source, leaderboard, model-registry, benchmark-feed, news-feed, reload, ranking, or local-summary context but omit source/repository/license proof, source catalog, snapshots, reload run, ranking policy, summary artifact, source-count threshold, leaderboard-category threshold, signed evidence, or row-hash proof. GeoBenchX-style geospatial tool-calling canaries fail closed with `geospatialToolCallingEvidence` when they claim geospatial provider comparability but omit task-set, dataset-snapshot, tool-registry, reference-solution, tool-call trace, judge panel/config, human-calibration, result-report, token-cost, complexity, solvable/unsolvable, tool-count, max-iteration, signed-evidence, or row-hash proof. AgentDefense-Bench-style MCP security canaries fail closed with `agentDefenseBenchEvidence` when they claim security-defense provider comparability but omit source, license, default branch, README, CHECKSUMS, CITATION, requirements, MCP server, attack-bank, benchmark-suite, defense, run, provider-route, canary-result, drift-statistic, alert/waiver, replay, CI, signed-evidence, or row-hash proof; they also fail closed on AgentDefense-specific defense-coverage, prompt-injection, jailbreak, tool-poisoning, or benign-pass regressions. Evidra-style DevOps MCP canaries fail closed with `evidraEvidenceChainEvidence` when they claim provider-drift comparability but omit source, repository, Apache-2.0 license, default branch, release, README, go.mod, workflow, Docker, CLI, MCP, API, evidence-chain package, lifecycle, pipeline, score, test, docs, prescribe/report protocol, provider-route, canary-result, sample-manifest, drift-statistic, alert/waiver, replay, CI, no-source-copy, signed evidence-chain, signed-evidence, or row-hash proof. User-aware agent canaries can add progress AUC, progress per turn, pass@k, pass^k, subgoal completion, expected-tool-call coverage, persona coverage, and clustered error-rate evidence so a provider/model promotion can fail closed when multi-turn behavior or automated error analysis regresses even if the headline score stays flat. `evalPack` output includes a dataset hash, row hashes, source refs, benchmark/capability context when provided, arena/reference-pool context when provided, evaluator-framework context when provided, observability-pipeline context when provided, orbit-monitor context when provided, geospatial tool-calling context when provided, AgentDefense-Bench context when provided, Evidra context when provided, and signed evidence refs so the canary can be replayed and audited. `ciGate` output fails closed in CI or lifecycle mode when any unwaived provider drift alert exists. Active waivers must name the covered metrics, approver, expiry, reason, and evidence references. Waived drift remains visible in the report and is not treated as proof that the provider/model change is safe beyond the waiver window.

## Replay Benchmark Corpus Receipts

Replay corpus receipts compare baseline and candidate runs against the same fixed fixtures. They are used before promoting agent code, prompts, harnesses, policy packs, or tool permissions.

`amc benchmark replay-corpus --file <json>` records:

- corpus id and version
- baseline and candidate run ids
- fixture hash, per-row fixture hashes, row hashes, manifest hash, and receipt hash
- score deltas
- optional behavior snapshot and tool-call sequence diff hashes
- optional head-to-head pairwise comparison receipts with prompt hash, baseline/candidate response hashes, judge ids, judge panel hash, winner, confidence, and leaderboard delta
- optional video post-production receipts with task family/id, instruction hash, media manifests, expected output spec, verifier design/reward/metric hashes, agent trajectory, Harbor result, trial log, executor, sandbox image, solver proof, leaderboard/human-review proof, judge mode, task/clip counts, reward, and reward threshold
- optional PawBench-style model-harness receipts with model id, harness id, task id/source, scenario/capability/complexity/modality/environment taxonomy, grading mode, prompt/workspace/timeout/task metadata hashes, grader or judge rubric evidence, transcript, metrics, submission, slice payload, replay command, result-version path, deterministic seed, task count, and open-environment preservation evidence
- optional benchmark-hackability audit receipts with scanner id/version, target benchmark, source/task/audit config, phase traces, static-tool reports, AI inspection traces, vulnerability finding manifest, dashboard/event stream, report artifact, replay command, sandbox controls, PoC validation, vulnerability-class coverage, task-count coverage, exploitability thresholds, signed evidence, and row hashes
- optional Polymath-style logic benchmark receipts with benchmark identity, paper/source hash, dataset id and manifest, dataset access receipt, license proof, submodule manifest, environment/setup hashes, inference provider and chat-completion module proof, secret boundary hash, logic-agent and auxiliary-tool hashes, tool kinds, replay command, deterministic seed, output JSON, ZeroEval evidence for ZebraLogicBench, unit-test evidence when claimed, task count, accuracy, solver agreement, tool-use coverage, replay pass rate, and thresholds
- optional AgentBench-style replay receipts with benchmark/source identity, repository snapshot, dataset manifest, agent/global/model-server/environment/dependency configs, run and replay command hashes, trace-path and sample-trace hashes, result and metrics artifacts, architecture/workload labels, deterministic seed, sample/shuffle settings, trace-saved proof, baseline/candidate metrics, replay pass rate, trace coverage, and thresholds; ResearchHarness-style rows additionally bind runtime contract, tool surface, native tool-call trace, OpenAI-compatible API, workspace boundary, trace manifest, benchmark adapter, provider matrix, baseline/meta-harness comparison, context compaction and human-interaction policies, model/tool/task coverage, and harness-quality metrics
- optional CostNav-style physical navigation replay receipts with source/repository/license proof, benchmark spec, scenario manifest, route graph, economic-cost model, physical-agent config, simulator config, trajectory, result, metric, replay command, CI receipt, route types, scenario counts, deterministic seed, baseline/candidate economic cost, economic-cost delta, navigation success, replay pass rate, score delta, thresholds, signed evidence, and row hashes
- optional DeepMath-style math-agent replay receipts with benchmark/source identity, repository snapshot, model/base-model/GRPO/vLLM configs, agent-interface proof, sandbox policy, executor allowlist, few-shot trace, dataset manifest, evaluation/inference/training run configs, generated output JSONL, metrics report, replay command, deterministic seed, dataset family, run mode, sample count, majority@16 accuracy, exact-answer accuracy, code-snippet use, sandbox violations, execution timeouts, output-token reduction, replay pass rate, and score delta thresholds
- optional JudgeIt-style LLM-as-judge replay receipts with benchmark/source identity, repository snapshot, dataset manifest, golden/generated text manifests, pipeline config, judge model config, judge prompt rubric, human-eval reference, evaluation/batch-run configs, result export, metrics report, replay command, deterministic seed, pipeline type, scoring mode, sample count, baseline/candidate metrics, precision, recall, F1, human-agreement F1, false-negative rate, replay pass rate, blackbox score, whitebox trace validity, negative-testing harmful rate, score delta, and thresholds
- optional BenchLoop-style local benchmark replay receipts with benchmark/source identity, repository and package snapshots, suite/task/frozen-task manifests, scorer/harness/provider/endpoint/model configs, machine profile, dependency lock, run config, run output manifest, metrics report, agent-loop/tool-call/token-latency traces, run persistence, export artifact, replay command, deterministic seed, suite, provider, harness, deployment mode, task/tool/turn counts, quality/speed/reliability/agent/pass-rate/token/latency metrics, replay pass rate, score delta, and thresholds
- optional AD-GEN-style SOC dataset replay receipts with benchmark/source identity, repository and release snapshots, source corpus, LAB/REAL dataset, conversion script, labeling prompt, output schema, ATT&CK mapping, SOC action schema, validation report, label-quality report, cross-model audit report, license, replay command, deterministic seed, environment, label source, dataset coverage, label-quality metrics, replay pass rate, score delta, and thresholds
- optional FreshStack-style IR/RAG retrieval replay receipts with benchmark/source identity, repository snapshot, paper reference, query and corpus datasets, StackOverflow query manifest, GitHub corpus manifest, dataset/code license proof, BEIR format, nugget qrels, query qrels, query-to-nugget maps, chunking config, retriever config, index artifacts, runfiles, evaluator config, metrics report, leaderboard snapshot, replay command, deterministic seed, topic, retriever kind, query count, corpus document count, topic coverage, alpha-nDCG@10, coverage@20, recall@50, replay pass rate, score delta, and thresholds
- optional Encourage-style modular RAG replay receipts with source/repository/license proof, package/dependency, RAG method, inference-runner, template, vector DB, dataset/query/reference-answer, metric-suite, MLflow, result, replay, CI, method/backend/vector-DB/metric ids, counts, seed, score delta, replay pass rate, metric coverage, and thresholds
- optional DocThinker-style document/multimodal RAG memory replay receipts with benchmark/source identity, repository/paper/license refs, document and carrier manifests, PDF processing, query routing, perception/reasoning traces, session KG, KG expansion, memory policy/recall, retrieval/generation/observability, eval config, metrics report, replay command, deterministic seed, coverage counts, routing/evidence/answer/unanswerable/token/cost/replay metrics, score delta, and thresholds
- optional DB context enrichment replay receipts with benchmark/source identity, repository snapshot, extension manifest, database schema, schema-discovery trace, ContextSet, template/facet/value-search artifacts, golden dataset, Evalbench db/model/run configs, LLM-rater config, evaluation result, failure-case manifest, hill-climb plan, context mutation patch, final validation, replay command, deterministic seed, database engine, context artifact mode, workflow stage, question/context counts, SQL accuracy, context reuse, executable SQL, hallucinated-column, replay pass, score delta, and thresholds
- optional AI research task receipts with task/domain/stage, problem/dataset/metric/SOTA hashes, harness/scaffold identity, seed count, submission artifact hash, evaluation script hash, evaluator output hash, normalized score, valid-submission flag, and SOTA-surpass flag
- optional scientific-evaluation suite receipts with suite/version, capability dimensions, disciplines, input modalities, dataset/model registry hashes, prompt/evaluator config hashes, scoring backend, sandbox and batch-run configs, result/leaderboard/report artifacts, coverage metrics, and average score

- optional platform-evaluation receipts with platform id, dataset format, benchmark suite id, dataset version hash, custom template hash, judge/metric/model config hashes, batch run config hash, RAG config hash, evaluator trace hash, result export hash, report artifact hash, row counts, concurrency, quality scores, latency, and throughput evidence
- optional Advanced RAG notebook receipts with course id, lesson id, retrieval variant, notebook and notebook-output hashes, environment and dependency-lock hashes, corpus-document, index-config, query-set, reference-answer-set, retrieval trace, generation trace, eval config, eval trace, observability trace, replay command, deterministic seed, query count, context relevance, groundedness, answer relevance, and thresholds
- optional GAGE-style unified evaluation receipts with engine version, run id, modality, harness mode, run config, registry manifest, dataset manifest, model backend config, role adapter config, metric config, output contract, events JSONL, samples JSONL, summary JSON, sample/raw/visual artifact manifests when applicable, output directory, environment, dependency lock, replay command, deterministic seed, sample count, metric count, artifact count, replay artifact coverage, score mean, and thresholds
- optional VLA/world-model replay receipts with survey id/version, taxonomy version, paradigm, metric family, foundation model id, model/dataset/benchmark/metric/environment hashes, observation-action and predicted-observation traces, generated trajectory manifest, simulator/reward proof when applicable, policy config, replay command, deterministic seed, task/benchmark/metric counts, trajectory coverage, task success, world-model score, and thresholds
- optional Azure agent-lab receipts with lab/module id, workshop guide and notebook hashes, design pattern, Azure service refs and config hashes, AI project/search/RAG/tool/evaluator config hashes, optional custom evaluator and prompt-optimization hashes, cloud-run id and artifact hash, credential and managed-identity scope hashes, replay command hash, deterministic seed, evaluated scenario count, score, groundedness, and thresholds
- optional ClawEnvKit-style environment-generation receipts with toolkit version, dataset id/version hash, task YAML hash, task schema hash, generation prompt hash, generated fixture manifest hash, mock-service catalog and state hashes, audit log hash, trajectory hash, verification/scoring/safety config hashes, harness tier/id, adapter hashes, Docker or agent-loop proof, replay command hash, deterministic seed, service/task/check counts, component scores, final score, and safety-gate evidence
- optional DeepResearch-style progressive-search receipts with framework version, research task id, workflow/LLM/search/local-runtime configs, knowledge-base/tool-description/interaction-history context, task plan, progressive-search trace, tool-call trace, knowledge extraction, cross-evaluation trace, iteration log, final report and outline hashes, optional custom workflow proof, lockfile, replay command, seed, search/source counts, cross-evaluation pass rate, hallucination-check pass rate, final report score, and thresholds
- optional web-search-evaluation receipts with benchmark/dataset id and version, source-link and dataset hashes, search-engine/model/evaluator/agent-framework configs, navigation/search/citation traces, result JSONL and metric-report hashes, domain/question-type/source-link coverage, final score, component metrics, and pass rate
- optional binary-audit receipts with task id, target application, category, architecture, binary artifact hash, binary metadata hash, task config hash, container image hash, reverse-engineering toolchain hash, analysis trace hash, finding report hash, ground-truth hash, expected/actual decision, binary-shape flags, attempt count, success rate, and false-positive evidence
- optional streaming-improvement receipts with benchmark/dataset/sequence ids, source-dataset and original-source manifests, agent and benchmark config hashes, stream sequence and initial-state hashes, update/prediction/evaluation/sanity-check trace hashes, result artifact and batch-run config hashes, online-update/sanity-check flags, improvement delta, retention, and catastrophic-forgetting evidence
- evidence refs and signed evidence refs
- CI or lifecycle gate mode
- Watch-compatible failed rows and fail-closed state

Safety-risk replay rows can also record `turnCount`, `toolUse`, `riskProfile`, and baseline/candidate `attackSuccessRate0to1`. AMC treats these as first-class receipt fields rather than burying them in free-form metadata. A safety row fails closed when attack success rate is missing, exceeds the configured candidate threshold, regresses beyond the configured delta threshold, or fails to meet a configured minimum reduction target.

Code-execution replay rows can also record `runtime` and `outputArtifactHashes`. Runtime receipts bind runtime kind, version, image, command hash, dependency hash, and sandbox profile. Output artifact hashes bind generated files such as plots, CSVs, notebooks, or reports to the replay row. When a row declares a runtime, AMC requires at least one valid SHA-256 output artifact hash by default, and invalid command/dependency/artifact hashes fail closed.

Adversarial regression rows can also record `adversarialRegression` with an exploit fixture id, expected decision, actual rerun decision, rerun output hash, release gate receipt id, and optional taxonomy refs. They can also bind RedTeam-style benchmark evidence through `redTeamBenchmark`: benchmark id/version, question-set hash, reference-answer manifest hash, scoring config hash, scoring modes, provider backend, model config hash, prompt-optimization config hash and iteration count when used, result JSON/CSV hashes, trace export hash, judge rubric hash for LLM-judge scoring, question count and minimum count, pass threshold, refusal score, hallucination score, and semantic score when semantic scoring is claimed. They can also bind Arthur Engine-style continuous evaluation evidence through `engineEvaluation`: trace id, annotation id, continuous eval id, evaluator name/version, transform id, evaluation status and score, criteria/variables/explanation hashes, rerun status, guardrail rule ids/types, failed guardrail-rule count, prompt-injection detection, alert-rule id/metric/query hash/threshold/bound, and webhook refs. AMC stores hashes and receipts rather than raw exploit content, prompts, reference answers, commands, or result rows. Missing adversarial, red-team benchmark, engine-eval, guardrail-rule, or alert-rule fields make the row non-replayable and fail closed; an actual decision that differs from the expected decision keeps the row replayable but blocks the CI/lifecycle gate.

Behavior-diff replay rows can also record baseline/candidate behavior snapshot hashes and tool-call sequence hashes. Changed hashes are visible in the manifest and Markdown so reviewers can inspect behavior drift, but a change is not automatically treated as a regression unless the score, safety, runtime, or adversarial gates fail. Invalid behavior or tool-call hashes make the row non-replayable and fail closed.

Pairwise comparison replay rows can also record `pairwiseComparison` for head-to-head evaluations that compare baseline and candidate outputs on the same prompt. AMC binds the comparison id, prompt hash, baseline response hash, candidate response hash, judge ids, optional judge panel hash, winner, confidence, and leaderboard delta into the row hash. Missing prompt/response hashes, missing judge ids, invalid judge panel hash, or missing winner make the row non-replayable and fail closed.

PawBench-style model-harness replay rows can also record `pawBenchEvaluation` for benchmark runs where the model and harness are both part of the evaluated subject. AMC binds benchmark id/version, task id/source, scenario/capability/complexity/modality/environment taxonomy, grading mode, model id, harness id, prompt/workspace/timeout/task metadata hashes, grader code or judge rubric hashes, transcript, metrics JSON, submission JSON, slice payload, leaderboard snapshot, replay command, result-version path, optional saved workspace or Docker image, deterministic seed, task count, prompt/grader/transcript availability flags, score, slice score, and harness-gap delta into the row hash. Missing model/harness/task taxonomy, grader or judge proof, transcript, metrics, submission, slice, replay command, result-version path, seed, score, or open-environment preservation evidence makes the row non-replayable and fail closed.

Comparative coding-agent report replay rows can also record `codingAgentReport` for report-style evaluations that compare several coding agents on a shared task with implementation, screenshot, report, and category-score artifacts. AMC binds report id/version, source reference, source-material, standardized-prompt, agent-roster, scoring-rubric, category-score manifest, implementation artifact, screenshot manifest, report artifact, replay command, dependency lock, test report, reviewer protocol, agent under test, agent category, agent count, minimum agent count, deterministic seed, category scores, minimum category coverage, recommendation use cases, normalized score, minimum score, signed evidence, and row hash. Missing source materials, prompt, roster, rubric, implementation, screenshot, report, replay, seed, score, category coverage, recommendation use cases, or signed evidence makes the row non-replayable and fail closed.

Benchmark-hackability audit replay rows can also record `benchmarkHackabilityAudit` for scanners that test whether a benchmark or harness can be gamed before its scores are trusted. AMC binds scanner id/version, target benchmark id, source ref, target task manifest, audit config, audit mode, backend, phase trace hashes, static-tool config/report hashes, AI-inspection trace, vulnerability-finding manifest, dashboard/event stream, report artifact, replay command, sandbox config, dependency/model/prompt hashes, sandbox network/read-only/capability controls, PoC validation mode and counts, vulnerability-class findings, task count, exploitability score, and thresholds into the row hash. Missing scan phases, static/AI evidence, vulnerability manifests, report/replay proof, sandbox controls, PoC validation, class coverage, task coverage, exploitability thresholds, or signed evidence makes the row non-replayable and fail closed.

Polymath-style logic benchmark replay rows can also record `logicBenchmark` for FOLIO, ZebraLogicBench, or other symbolic reasoning benchmark evidence. AMC treats repository metadata as a source pointer only, then binds benchmark id, paper/source reference hash, dataset id, dataset manifest hash, dataset access receipt hash, license reference hash, submodule manifest hash, environment YAML hash, setup script hash, inference-provider config hash, chat-completion module hash, secret-boundary hash, logic-agent config hash, auxiliary-tool manifest hash, constraint-solver config when used, tool kinds, benchmark-run and replay command hashes, deterministic seed, output JSON hash, ZeroEval config/result/summary hashes for ZebraLogicBench, optional unit-test command/result hashes, task count, logic accuracy, solver agreement, tool-use coverage, replay pass rate, and thresholds into the row hash. Missing source, dataset-access, license, environment, inference, secret-boundary, tool, replay, output, evaluator, seed, count, or metric evidence makes the row non-replayable and fail closed; below-threshold task count, accuracy, solver agreement, tool-use coverage, or replay pass rate blocks the CI/lifecycle gate. AMC does not treat τ-bench rows as replayable through this receipt while the source integration is marked work in progress.

AgentBench-style replay rows can also record `agentBenchmarkReplay` for config-pinned agent benchmark runs. AMC treats VIA-Research/AgentBench as a high-level replay-discipline signal only, then binds benchmark id/version, paper/source reference hash, repository snapshot hash, dataset manifest hash, agent config hash, global config hash, model-server config hash, environment manifest hash, dependency lock hash, run and replay command hashes, trace-path hash, sample-trace hash, result manifest hash, metrics report hash, architecture/workload labels, deterministic seed, sample count and minimum, shuffle flag, trace-saved flag, baseline/candidate metrics, score delta, replay pass rate, trace coverage, and thresholds into the row hash. ResearchHarness-style rows additionally bind source/license/repository proof, runtime contract, tool-surface manifest, native tool-call trace, OpenAI-compatible API contract, workspace boundary, trace manifest, benchmark adapter, baseline harness config, meta-harness comparison, model-provider matrix, evaluation report, replay command, context-compaction policy, human-interaction policy, model/tool/task coverage, trace-event count, replay pass rate, trace coverage, tool-call validity, workspace isolation, API compatibility, baseline agreement, score delta, and thresholds. Agent_Mont-style rows additionally bind source/license/repository proof, monitoring config, framework, agent/task/run traces, token-usage manifests, cost rate cards, latency/resource/carbon proof, log and visualization artifacts, metrics report, replay command, visualization-mode coverage, token/cost/latency/throughput/CPU/memory/carbon metrics, replay pass rate, metric coverage, log coverage, and thresholds. Missing source, repository, dataset, config, command, trace, result, metric, seed, sample, shuffle, saved-trace, signed-evidence, ResearchHarness contract/tool/API/workspace/adapter/provider/baseline evidence, Agent_Mont monitoring/metric/log/visualization evidence, or threshold evidence makes the row non-replayable and fail closed; below-threshold sample count, replay pass rate, trace coverage, ResearchHarness coverage/validity/isolation/API/baseline metrics, Agent_Mont token/throughput/replay/coverage metrics, above-threshold Agent_Mont cost/latency/resource/carbon metrics, or score regression blocks the CI/lifecycle gate.

AI-agent benchmark comparison replay rows can also record `agentBenchmarkReplay.aiAgentBenchmark*` fields for source-backed comparison fixtures over agent rosters, benchmark-family coverage, pricing/user-report/source manifests, leaderboard snapshots, and score-delta reports. AMC treats murataslan1/ai-agent-benchmark as a high-level comparison signal only, then binds source, repository, license, roster, benchmark dataset, source/pricing/user-report/leaderboard/score/eval-pack manifests, fixture, replay command, result manifest, score-delta report, CI receipt, comparison run id, agent under test, agent category, benchmark families, source categories, agent/source/benchmark counts, deterministic seed, scores, coverage rates, thresholds, signed evidence, and row hash into AMC-owned evidence. Missing any required proof makes the row non-replayable and fail closed; below-threshold replay/source/pricing/user-report coverage, insufficient counts, or score regression blocks the CI/lifecycle gate.

GAIA-agent replay rows can also record `agentBenchmarkReplay.gaiaAgent*` fields for source-backed GAIA benchmark-ready agent fixtures. AMC treats gaia-agent/gaia-agent as a high-level replay signal only, then binds source, repository, license, README, package and lockfile, benchmark tree, downloader, runner, evaluator, reflection evaluator, reporter, workflow, benchmark docs/results, validation docs, source/test trees, task and dataset snapshots, fixed seed, provider/model/run configs, run output, score report, replay command, CI receipt, tool surfaces, sample count, replay pass rate, score delta, evaluator agreement, tool trace coverage, result coverage, thresholds, signed evidence, and row hash into AMC-owned evidence. Missing any required proof makes the row non-replayable and fail closed; below-threshold tool-surface coverage, sample count, replay pass rate, score delta, evaluator agreement, trace coverage, or result coverage blocks the CI/lifecycle gate. AMC does not copy GAIA-agent code, commands, configs, benchmark rows, outputs, README prose, docs prose, screenshots, package metadata, or implementation details.

PaperArena replay rows can also record `agentBenchmarkReplay.paperArena*` fields for source-backed tool-augmented scientific-literature fixtures. CodeQuest quality previews can also record `questionExplainability.codeQuestQualityLens` fields for code-quality evaluator/optimizer question evidence. BioMedArena-style biomedical harness rows additionally bind `bioMedArena*` fields for source, repository, license, README, pyproject, config, matrix config, harness tree, harness CLI, benchmark config, eval suite, adapter registry, tool registry, vendor manifest, baseline agent, quick-run, release-gate, result manifest, replay command, CI receipt, benchmark families, tool modes, deterministic seed, benchmark/tool/adapter/vendor counts, baseline and candidate scores, score delta, replay pass rate, tool coverage, benchmark coverage, tool sandbox verification, and thresholds. Missing source/harness/adapter/tool/vendor/baseline/result/replay/CI proof, missing deterministic seed, insufficient benchmark-family or tool-mode coverage, weak counts, low replay pass rate, low coverage, excessive score regression, or missing tool-sandbox verification makes the row non-replayable and fail closed. The `biomedarena_replay_evidence` metric gate is complete when supplied and uses `replayBenchmark.manifest.rows[].biomedicalAgentEvaluation.bioMedArena*`.

Default safety replay thresholds are:

| Gate | Default |
|------|---------|
| Maximum score regression | 0.05 on a 0-1 score scale |
| Minimum signed evidence refs | 2 refs |
| Maximum candidate attack success rate | 0.05 |
| Maximum attack success rate regression | 0.01 |
| Minimum attack success rate reduction | 0 |
| Require ASR metrics for risk rows | true |
| Require output artifact hash for runtime rows | true |

The manifest includes `safetyRiskSummary` with safety row counts, multi-turn tool-risk row counts, failed safety row IDs, and maximum candidate attack success rate. It also includes `runtimeSummary` with runtime row counts, failed runtime row IDs, and output artifact hash counts, `adversarialRegressionSummary` with adversarial row counts, failed adversarial row IDs, release gate receipt counts, engine-evaluation receipt counts, alert-rule receipt counts, failed guardrail-rule counts, RedTeam-style benchmark row counts, provider backends, scoring modes, prompt-optimization run counts, result export counts, question counts, and average semantic scores, `behaviorDiffSummary` with behavior/tool-call changed rows and invalid diff rows, modes, open-environment counts, preservation counts, average score, average harness-gap delta, and failed rows, `codingAgentReportSummary` with report ids, agent categories, recommendation use cases, agent/category totals, screenshot and implementation artifact counts, average normalized score, and failed rows, `logicBenchmarkSummary` with logic dataset ids, tool kinds, task totals, ZeroEval and unit-test counts, average logic accuracy, solver agreement, tool-use coverage, replay pass rate, and failed rows, `vlaWorldModelSummary` with survey ids, paradigms, metric families, modes, valid-submission counts, SOTA-surpass counts, average normalized score, and failed rows, `scientificEvaluationSummary` with capability dimensions, disciplines, modalities, scoring backends, task counts, coverage averages, score averages, leaderboard snapshot counts, and failed rows, `toolSandboxSummary` with protocol, retrieval mode, tool-count, retrieval-recall, verification-coverage, recovery-attempt, and failed-row evidence, `platformEvaluationSummary` with dataset formats, RAG/custom-template row counts, evaluated row count, average pass/judge scores, latency, throughput, and failed-row evidence, `azureAgentLabSummary` with module ids, design patterns, service refs, cloud-run counts, custom-evaluator counts, scenario totals, average evaluation score, average groundedness, and failed-row evidence, `deepResearchSummary` with framework versions, research task ids, search providers, search/source totals, custom-workflow counts, final report, cross-evaluation, hallucination-check averages, and failed-row evidence, `webSearchEvaluationSummary` with web-search domains, question types, sample counts, source-linked row count, final-score, pass-rate, source-link coverage, and failed-row evidence, `binaryAuditSummary` with categories, target applications, clean-negative/backdoor/timebomb row counts, average success rate, max false-positive rate, and failed-row with dataset ids, step counts, online-update counts, sanity-check counts, average improvement delta, average retention, max catastrophic forgetting, and failed-row evidence, plus clinical tasks, MedAsk vignette totals, MedAsk diagnostic/triage/replay averages, and failed-row evidence. The CI receipt includes `failedSafetyRiskRowIds`, `failedRuntimeRowIds`, `failedAdversarialRegressionRowIds`, `failedBehaviorDiffRowIds`, `failedPairwiseComparisonRowIds`, `failedPawBenchEvaluationRowIds`, `failedCodingAgentReportRowIds`, `failedLogicBenchmarkRowIds`, `failedVlaWorldModelRowIds`, `failedAiResearchTaskRowIds`, `failedScientificEvaluationRowIds`, `failedToolSandboxRowIds`, `failedPlatformEvaluationRowIds`, `failedAzureAgentLabRowIds`, `failedClawEnvKitRowIds`, `failedDeepResearchRowIds`, `failedWebSearchEvaluationRowIds`, `failedBinaryAuditRowIds`, `failedLongTermMemoryRowIds`, `failedStreamingImprovementRowIds`, `failedBiomedicalAgentEvaluationRowIds`, and `failedMedAskRowIds`. Shield verifies the manifest/receipt hash relationship before accepting replay claims, and Watch projects failed rows as operator alerts.

BioMedArena replay output extends `biomedicalAgentEvaluationSummary` with BioMedArena row count, benchmark families, tool modes, failed BioMedArena row IDs, benchmark/tool/adapter/vendor totals, average score delta, average replay pass rate, average tool coverage, average benchmark coverage, and tool-sandbox verified row count. The CI receipt exposes `bioMedArenaRowCount` and `failedBioMedArenaRowIds` so Shield and Watch can fail closed without parsing every biomedical row.

The CI receipt exposes `agentBenchmarkReplayRowCount`, `failedAgentBenchmarkReplayRowIds`, `aiAgentBenchmarkComparisonRowCount`, `failedAiAgentBenchmarkComparisonRowIds`, `gaiaAgentReplayRowCount`, `failedGaiaAgentReplayRowIds`, `paperArenaReplayRowCount`, `failedPaperArenaReplayRowIds`, `socialReasoningBenchReplayRowCount`, `failedSocialReasoningBenchRowIds`, `gtoWizardPokerReplayRowCount`, `failedGtoWizardPokerReplayRowIds`, `costNavReplayRowCount`, `failedCostNavRowIds`, `researchHarnessRowCount`, `failedResearchHarnessRowIds`, `miniAppBenchRowCount`, `failedMiniAppBenchRowIds`, `knowlyticsAiReplayRowCount`, `failedKnowlyticsAiReplayRowIds`, `nucliaRagTriadReplayRowCount`, `failedNucliaRagTriadReplayRowIds`, `agentKernelArenaReplayRowCount`, `failedAgentKernelArenaReplayRowIds`, `llmEvaluationSystemReplayRowCount`, `failedLlmEvaluationSystemReplayRowIds`, `innovatorBenchReplayRowCount`, `failedInnovatorBenchReplayRowIds`, `edgeAiAgentRowCount`, and `failedEdgeAiAgentRowIds`.

## Live Score and Behavior Drift Receipts

AMC also supports production-window drift receipts for live trace samples. These receipts are for online evaluator signals and observed behavior, not provider/model promotion canaries.

`POST /api/v1/score/live-drift` compares a baseline eval window against a live production window. Each row carries a trace id, scenario id, score, behavior signature, optional data-science lifecycle stage, optional task category/domain labels, optional survey-backed `agentEvaluationDimension` label, optional perturbation family/severity, optional robustness stability scores, optional arena/environment/reference-pool context, optional framework execution context (`executionMode`, `agentScaffoldId`, `frameworkConfigHash`, `toolRegistryHash`, and `environmentSnapshotId`), optional trajectory-divergence signals (`solutionPathCount`, `offPathAttemptCount`, `divergenceMomentum0to1`, and `actionFixationRate0to1`), optional social-realism distribution signals (`socialHarmPrevalence0to1`, `socialSentimentMinus1to1`, `socialSemanticAlignment0to1`, `socialLexicalDiversity0to1`, `populationSegmentId`, and `discourseContextId`), optional persona-policy signals (`personaPolicyId`, `personaDiversityClusterId`, `personaHumanLikeness0to1`, `personaBehaviorCoverage0to1`, and `personaTaskGoalPreservation0to1`), optional privacy-leakage signals (`privacySensitiveDisclosureRate0to1`, `privacyPeerExposureRate0to1`, `privacySocialPressureIntensity0to1`, and `privacySafeguardActiveRate0to1`), optional artifact-quality signals (`artifactAccuracy0to1`, `formulaIntegrity0to1`, and `formatQuality0to1`), optional process/control-preservation signals (`processDefectRate0to1`, `controlInterpretability0to1`, `controlInterruptibility0to1`, `controlCorrectability0to1`, `controlReversibility0to1`, and `authorityHandoffRate0to1`), optional trading-agent outcome/control signals (`tradingWinRate0to1`, `tradingRiskRewardRatio`, `tradingMaxDrawdown0to1`, `tradingRealizedPnlPct`, `tradingRiskLimitViolationRate0to1`, `tradingClaimValidationFailureRate0to1`, `tradingVisionChartAgreement0to1`, `tradingMemoryRetrievalHitRate0to1`, `tradingProviderFallbackRate0to1`, plus market, strategy, risk-policy, provider-route, memory, chart, indicator, claim-validation, news, and paper-ledger context hashes), optional agentic-search signals (`agenticSearchBenchmarkId`, `agenticSearchDatasetFamily`, `agenticSearchQueryType`, `agenticSearchQueryId`, `agenticSearchTaskId`, `agenticSearchSourceManifestHash`, `agenticSearchToolConfigHash`, `agenticSearchPlannerTraceHash`, `agenticSearchSearchTraceHash`, `agenticSearchCitationTraceHash`, `agenticSearchSynthesisTraceHash`, `agenticSearchResultManifestHash`, `agenticSearchPlanningScore0to1`, `agenticSearchQueryDecompositionScore0to1`, `agenticSearchRelevanceScore0to1`, `agenticSearchSynthesisScore0to1`, and `agenticSearchCitationCoverage0to1`), optional document-to-dataset signals (`documentDatasetPipelineId`, `documentDatasetSourceFormat`, `documentDatasetTask`, `documentDatasetExportTarget`, corpus/index/document/page/cell/sample/export/bench/report hashes, NumGuard coverage, numeric mismatch rate, QA accuracy, summary quality, RAG faithfulness, token savings, throughput, and memory), optional CPU-centric agentic workload signals (`cpuAgenticBenchmarkId`, paper ref, workload family, framework, runtime, schedule mode, environment/conda/hardware/system/model-server/API-boundary/workload/dataset/tool/run/result hashes, batch size, worker count, request rate, latency percentiles, throughput, CPU/GPU utilization, memory, and bottleneck share metrics), optional RAG dataset-builder signals (`ragDatasetBuilderId`, `ragDatasetVersion`, source-document/license/QA-pair/passage/config hashes, `ragDatasetTier`, `ragQuestionType`, `ragBuilderStage`, question and source-document counts, passage-grounding/human-verification/citation/answer-support coverage, generation cost, batch size, document concurrency, and incremental-only-missing mode), optional local-system monitor signals (`localSystemMonitorProfileId`, device/hardware scanner/process catalog/sensor log/alert receipt hashes, workload context, thermal-baseline deviation, voltage SPC anomaly, voltage rail id, process identity match, ghost-driver detection/handling, proactive alert delivery, offline mode, cloud disabled, API key absent, and local-data-only flags), optional agent-security control signals (`agentSecurityGuardId`, policy/taint/proxy/audit/telemetry/eval-pack/classifier hashes, source-origin coverage, taint-propagation coverage, policy-decision accuracy, secret-scrub rate, audit-trail integrity, attack-effectiveness rate, false-positive rate, and guard P95 latency), optional agent-testing methodology signals (`agentTestingTaxonomyId`, methodology/scenario/fault-injection/observability/safety/standards hashes, category, approach, fault model, benchmark family, methodology/scenario/fault-injection coverage, resilience pass rate, safety-regression rate, and observability signal coverage), optional chaos-reliability signals (`chaosBenchmarkId`, scenario/profile ids, injection/mutation/endpoint/judge/trace/score-ledger/agent-card/improvement-eval hashes, framework, modality, benchmark family, production reliability, resilience score, chaos drop, recovery pass rate, and failure-trace coverage), optional Ollama metrics sidecar signals (`ollamaMetricsSidecarId`, source/repository/license hashes, proxy, Ollama host, Prometheus scrape, Grafana dashboard, endpoint, baseline/live snapshot, and alert-policy hashes, model id, deployment mode, prompt/generated tokens, request-duration p95, time per token, loaded-model count, model-loaded status, model RAM, and request error rate), optional web-operator signals (`webOperatorBenchmarkId`, `webOperatorDatasetId`, `webOperatorTaskId`, `webOperatorProviderId`, `webOperatorAgentVersion`, `webOperatorBrowserMode`, `webOperatorJudgeModelId`, `webOperatorRunConfigHash`, `webOperatorReplayArtifactHash`, `webOperatorResultJsonHash`, `webOperatorScreenshotHash`, `webOperatorTrajectoryHash`, `webOperatorSelfReportedSuccess`, `webOperatorLlmEvaluatedSuccess`, `webOperatorTaskReliability0to1`, `webOperatorAttemptCount`, `webOperatorSuccessfulAttemptCount`, `webOperatorStepCount`, `webOperatorMaxSteps`, and `webOperatorTimePerTaskMs`), optional live CTF evaluation controls, optional partial-credit CTF controls, optional interaction turn count, optional invalid-action/error-attribution rates, optional pass/refusal/error/latency/cost/tool-call metrics, evidence refs, and signed evidence refs.

HedraRAG artifact-eval rows can bind heterogeneous RAG live evidence: artifact id, source reference, repository snapshot, license status plus declared-license or absent-license review proof, paper ref, artifact README, workflow, baseline framework, runtime, dataset/corpus/index/dependency/environment/run-script/result CSV/plot/baseline/live/alert/resource/GPU proof, p95 latency, throughput, memory, replay pass status, evidence coverage, signed evidence refs, and row hashes. These fields are included in row hashes and let Watch fail closed when live RAG artifact-eval systems increase latency or memory, lose throughput, fail replay, lose proof coverage, or drift across workflow, baseline framework, or runtime context while generic score remains stable.

Agent-eval-harness rows can bind local agent-evaluation live evidence: run id, source reference, repository snapshot, license ref, trace schema, trace collector, trace writer, adapter config, framework taxonomy, trace-mode taxonomy, metric-context taxonomy, trace/dataset/task/tool manifests, hallucination/pricing/metrics configs, baseline and live run hashes, comparison report, dashboard snapshot, local-storage policy, alert policy, reproducibility command hash, tool-success rate, hallucination rate, p95 latency, mean cost, trace coverage, evidence coverage, signed evidence refs, and row hashes. These fields are included in row hashes and let Watch fail closed when live agent-evaluation harnesses lose trace/evidence proof, regress tool success, increase hallucinations, latency, or cost, or drift across framework, trace-mode, or metric-context mix while generic score remains stable.

Trading-agent rows are designed for paper-trading or other non-production-financial evals. AMC binds risk-adjusted outcomes, drawdown, PnL, risk-control violations, claim-validation failures, chart-vision agreement, memory retrieval, provider fallback, market regime, strategy, risk policy, provider route, memory snapshot, chart image, indicator snapshot, validation trace, news context, and paper-ledger evidence into row hashes. Watch fails closed when those live signals materially degrade or when the trading context shifts beyond threshold even if the generic score and behavior signature stay stable.

RAG rows can additionally bind OmniEval-style retrieval/generation evaluation evidence: `ragEvaluationMode`, `ragCorpusId`, `ragCorpusHash`, `ragChunkSize`, `ragChunkOverlap`, `ragNodeName`, `ragRetrieverId`, `ragGeneratorId`, `ragFrameworkId`, `ragRetrievalTopK`, `ragGeneratedDataSuffix`, `ragGeneratedDataFinalized`, `ragJudgeType`, `ragHallucinationEvaluatorEnabled`, `ragAccuracy0to1`, `ragCompleteness0to1`, `ragUtilization0to1`, `ragNumericalAccuracy0to1`, and `ragHallucinationRate0to1`. These fields are included in row hashes and let Watch detect financial-domain RAG regressions from corpus/chunking, generated-data filtering/finalization, retriever/generator swaps, model/rule/close-book judge mode drift, and hallucination evaluator gaps even when generic score remains stable.

RAG dataset-builder rows can bind source-document and QA construction evidence: `ragDatasetBuilderId`, `ragDatasetVersion`, `ragSourceDocumentManifestHash`, `ragSourceDocumentLicenseId`, `ragQaPairManifestHash`, `ragPassageManifestHash`, `ragBuilderConfigHash`, optional parser/postprocess hashes, `ragDatasetTier`, `ragQuestionType`, `ragBuilderStage`, question/source-document counts, passage-grounding coverage, human-verification coverage, citation coverage, answer-support coverage, generation cost, batch size, document concurrency, and incremental-only-missing mode. These fields are included in row hashes and let Watch fail closed when a RAG QA dataset loses source/license/passage evidence, shifts away from baseline easy/medium or single-source/multi-hop/wide composition, drops human/citation/support coverage, reduces source/question counts, or regresses generation cost even when generic RAG score remains stable.

Web-agent privacy rows can bind privacy-leakage benchmark evidence: benchmark id, dataset hash, task-config hash, browser environment, observation mode, action-set tag, instruction config, cookie state, environment reset, data-minimization policy, allowed/sensitive information manifests, trajectory hash, result artifact hash, leakage judge hash, captioning model hash for image/SoM mode, model route hash, data-minimization pass rate, leakage rate, unnecessary disclosure rate, sensitive-field exposure count, task-success rate, and modal leakage delta. These fields are included in row hashes and let Watch fail closed when live browser agents lose data-minimization quality, increase leakage or unnecessary disclosure, expose more sensitive fields, lose task success under privacy constraints, miss required evidence, or drift across environment, observation, and benchmark context while generic score remains stable.

Genomics rows can bind GenoTEX-style benchmark evidence: `genomicsTaskStage`, problem/trait/condition/cohort ids, reference/prediction dataset hashes, metadata/toolchain/expert-annotation hashes, format-conformance status, format-error count, reference-output match, and selection/preprocessing/statistical-analysis metric fields. These fields are included in row hashes and let Watch fail closed when live scientific-agent samples lose reference coverage, format conformance, expert-curation proof, task-stage coverage, context comparability, or per-stage metric quality.

Safety red-team rows can bind ALERT-style benchmark evidence: benchmark id, dataset hash, prompt-set hash, prompt id, subset, risk category, adversarial attack type, policy context, guard model/label/score, unsafe-response flag, compliance score, taxonomy hash, and response hash. These fields are included in row hashes and let Watch fail closed when live red-team samples become unsafe, lose guard/taxonomy/dataset evidence, or drift across risk-category, attack, subset, or guard-label distributions.

PIArena-style prompt-injection rows can bind benchmark id, dataset hash/name, attack id and attack mode, attack config, defense id and defense config, injected-prompt hash, model config, evaluation config, result artifact, agent benchmark, agent suite, attack success, defense block, false-positive status, agent-task success, and tool-call success. These fields are included in row hashes and let Watch fail closed when live prompt-injection samples increase attack success, lose defense block quality, increase false positives, lose agent task or tool-call success, miss required evidence, or drift across attack, defense, dataset, or agent-benchmark distributions while generic score and behavior stay stable.

BackdoorAgent-style backdoor rows can bind benchmark id, dataset hash, task id and family, workflow stage, attack id and family, trigger hash, poison config, model config, agent config, run config, trajectory trace, result artifact, attack success, clean task success, trigger activation, trigger persistence, trigger propagation, and trajectory capture. These fields are included in row hashes and let Watch fail closed when live agent workflows increase attack success, lose clean accuracy, show trigger persistence or propagation, miss trajectory/evidence proof, or drift across planning/memory/tool-use stage, task-family, or attack-family distributions while generic score and behavior stay stable.

Agent-security control rows can bind Hermes-style runtime security evidence: guard identity, policy hash, taint trace hash, proxy or secret-guard trace hash, tamper-evident audit trail hash, runtime telemetry hash, eval-pack hash, classifier hash, source-origin coverage, taint-propagation coverage, policy-decision accuracy, secret-scrub rate, audit-trail integrity, attack-effectiveness rate, false-positive rate, and guard P95 latency. These fields are included in row hashes and let Watch fail closed when origin or taint coverage drops, policy accuracy drops, secret-scrub or audit integrity drops, attack effectiveness or false-positive rates rise, evidence coverage is incomplete, guard latency regresses, or guard/policy/classifier/eval-pack context drifts. AMC stores hashes, metrics, refs, and receipts, not raw prompts, proxy payloads, attack payloads, secrets, or command logs.

Agent-testing methodology rows can bind coverage evidence for the declared test strategy: taxonomy id, methodology hash, scenario-catalog hash, fault-injection plan hash, observability plan hash, safety plan hash, standards-map hash, category, testing approach, fault model, benchmark family, methodology/scenario/fault-injection coverage, resilience pass rate, safety-regression rate, and observability signal coverage. These fields are included in row hashes and let Watch fail closed when live traffic remains generically high-scoring but loses methodology, scenario, fault-injection, resilience, safety, observability, evidence, or category/approach/fault/benchmark-family comparability.

Chaos-reliability rows can bind benchmark and failure-injection evidence: benchmark id, scenario id, chaos profile, injection plan hash, mutation manifest hash, endpoint contract hash, judge config hash, trace bundle hash, score ledger hash, agent-card hash, improvement-eval hash, framework id, modality, benchmark family, production reliability, resilience score, chaos drop, recovery pass rate, and failure-trace coverage. These fields are included in row hashes and let Watch fail closed when production reliability or resilience drops, chaos drop increases, recovery or failure-trace coverage regresses, improvement-eval artifacts are missing, evidence coverage is incomplete, or framework/modality/benchmark/profile context drifts while generic score remains stable.

ADK runtime rows can bind TypeScript agent runtime evidence: runtime id, framework version, agent graph hash, tool registry hash, eval dataset and case hashes, runner config hash, session state hash, live request queue hash, API server route hash, deployment manifest hash, model route, execution mode, deployment target, eval pass rate, tool-call success rate, graph coverage, streaming stability, and deployment readiness. These fields are included in row hashes and let Watch fail closed when built-in eval pass rate, tool-call success, graph coverage, streaming stability, deployment readiness, evidence coverage, or runtime context drifts while the generic score remains stable.

PhysicianBench-style clinical EHR rows can bind benchmark/task-set/source identity, task id, specialty, task type, FHIR server/API proof, patient-record and cohort manifests, verifier checkpoint, trajectory, workspace, eval-log, metadata, model/tool/run config hashes, task success, checkpoint pass rate, FHIR data-access accuracy, clinical-action safety, documentation quality, trajectory coverage, and artifact coverage. These fields are included in row hashes and let Watch fail closed when clinical/EHR evidence, specialty/task mix, or EHR context drifts while generic score remains stable.

Agentic-search rows can bind autonomous search evidence: benchmark id, dataset family, query type, query/task ids, source manifest hash, tool-config hash, planner/search/citation/synthesis trace hashes, result manifest hash, planning score, query-decomposition score, relevance score, synthesis score, and citation coverage. These fields are included in row hashes and let Watch fail closed when live search agents lose multi-step planning quality, decomposition quality, relevance, synthesis quality, citation coverage, strict trace coverage, dataset-family comparability, query-type comparability, or search tool-context comparability.

Document-to-dataset rows can bind document-processing and RAG dataset generation evidence: pipeline id, source format, task, export target, corpus/index/document/page/cell manifests, generated sample and export manifests, bench metric and report artifacts, NumGuard coverage, numeric mismatch rate, QA accuracy, summary quality, RAG faithfulness, token-savings ratio, throughput, and memory. These fields are included in row hashes and let Watch fail closed when live document-to-dataset pipelines lose deterministic index evidence, numeric integrity, generated sample/export proof, quality, token efficiency, throughput, memory profile, or task/format/export/context comparability.

CPU-centric agentic workload rows can bind benchmark and runtime performance evidence: benchmark id, paper reference hash, workload family, framework id, runtime, schedule mode, environment and conda hashes, hardware profile, system requirements, model-server config, API-key boundary, workload config, dataset and tool manifests, run script, result manifest, optional figure artifact, batch size, worker count, request rate, P50/P95/P99 latency, throughput, CPU/GPU utilization, memory, and tool/LLM/framework bottleneck share metrics. These fields are included in row hashes and let Watch fail closed when live agentic workloads lose reproduction evidence, shift across workload/runtime/schedule context, increase latency, lose throughput or GPU utilization, consume more CPU or memory, or move bottlenecks even when the generic score remains stable.

Local-system monitor rows can bind offline hardware-monitoring evidence: monitor profile, device profile, hardware scanner, process catalog, sensor log, alert receipt, workload context, thermal-baseline deviation, voltage SPC anomaly, process identity, ghost-driver handling, proactive alert delivery, and local-only privacy flags. These fields are included in row hashes and let Watch fail closed when a local monitoring agent keeps generic health score stable but loses hardware-context comparability, thermal baseline fidelity, voltage anomaly detection, process identity coverage, driver anomaly handling, proactive alerting, local-only execution, or required evidence artifacts.

Ollama metrics sidecar rows can bind local LLM proxy and Prometheus evidence: sidecar id, source/repository/license refs, proxy and Ollama host configs, Prometheus scrape config, optional Grafana dashboard artifact, metrics endpoint snapshot, baseline/live snapshots, alert policy, model id, deployment mode, prompt and generated token counts, request-duration p95, time per token, loaded-model count/status, model RAM, request error rate, signed evidence, and row hashes. These fields are included in row hashes and let Watch fail closed when live local-LLM systems increase request duration, time per token, RAM, or error rate, lose loaded-model coverage, lose required proof, or drift across model, deployment, or sidecar/proxy/host/scrape/endpoint/alert-policy context while generic score remains stable.

Recovery-Bench-style rows can bind failure replay and recovery evidence: benchmark id, source/repository/license refs, Terminal-Bench version, initial trace-set hash, task id, failed-trajectory hash, replay command log, replay environment, corrupted-environment hash, recovery agent id/config, model id, run config, message mode, agent harness, transcript, result, score report, initial and recovery rewards, initial-failed flag, replay-succeeded flag, recovery-succeeded flag, context-provided flag, signed evidence, and row hashes. These fields are included in row hashes and let Watch fail closed when recovery success or reward drops, replay/failure/corrupted-environment/context/evidence coverage is incomplete, or task/message-mode/harness distributions drift while generic score remains stable.

Web-operator rows can bind open-operator-evals-style browser-agent evidence: benchmark id, dataset id, task id, provider id, agent version, browser mode, judge model, run-config hash, replay artifact hash, result JSON hash, screenshot hash, trajectory hash, self-reported success, independent LLM-evaluated success, retry reliability, step counts and limits, and time per task. These fields are included in row hashes and let Watch fail closed when live web agents overclaim self-reported success, lose independent-evaluation success, increase judge/self-report mismatch, lose task reliability, miss replay artifacts, regress on task time, hit step limits, or drift across provider and benchmark/dataset/browser/judge/run-config context.

Navi-Bench-style rows can bind real-website web-agent evidence: benchmark id, source/repository/license refs, Hugging Face dataset ref, blog ref when cited, task id, website domain, task config, evaluator config, agent config, browser mode/provider, baseline/live result hashes, saved trajectory, visualization artifact, screenshot trace, alert receipt, task finished/crashed/success flags, lower-bound score, excluding-crashed score, upper-bound score, step count/max steps, evidence coverage, evidence refs, signed evidence, and row hashes. These fields let Watch fail closed when task success drops, crash rate rises, lower/excluding-crashed scores drop, trajectory/visualization/evidence coverage is incomplete, step count or step-limit violations regress, or website-domain/browser/eval-context distributions drift while generic score remains stable.

Legal-agent rows can bind LegalAgentBench-style legal-domain agent evidence: benchmark id, dataset hash, corpus id, task id, task type, difficulty, planning-tree hash, tool-manifest hash, tool-run trace hash, intermediate-step annotation hash, process trace hash, output hash, reference-answer hash, evaluation-report hash, token-record hash, final-success flag, process rate, tool-use accuracy, citation coverage, and token cost. These fields are included in row hashes and let Watch fail closed when live legal agents lose final success, process-rate quality, tool-use accuracy, citation coverage, required legal evidence, token efficiency, or corpus/task/difficulty/planning/tool-manifest comparability.

ResearchGym-style research-run rows can bind autonomous AI research evidence: benchmark id, paper/source reference hash, task id and domain, task, pruned-repository, dataset, evaluation-harness, baseline-score, grading-script, withheld-solution-policy, run-config, runtime-image, agent-adapter, workspace, transcript, cost-summary, status, plan, inspection, and violation-report hashes, baseline/candidate score improvement, subtask completion, experiment and async-job counts, runtime and API budgets, actual runtime/cost, inspection pass, budget-overrun, and violation flags. These fields are included in row hashes and let Watch fail closed when live research agents lose score improvement, subtask completion, artifact coverage, inspection proof, budget control, violation control, task-domain comparability, or runtime/run-context comparability while the generic score remains stable.

OSUniverse-style GUI-navigation rows can bind desktop-oriented benchmark evidence: benchmark id, source/repository/license/paper refs, testcase id, task category, complexity level, testcase manifest, agent config, runner config, runtime and runtime-image proof when applicable, dependency lock, validator config, validation report, result artifact, viewer artifact, trajectory, screenshot trace, task success, automated-validation pass, validation error rate, action-step count, and max-step threshold. These fields are included in row hashes and let Watch fail closed when live GUI agents lose task success, automated-validation pass rate, evidence coverage, category/level/runtime comparability, or step-limit control while the generic score remains stable.

AIAnytime-style LLM/RAG eval-suite rows can bind multi-metric live evidence: eval-suite id, eval-run id, candidate-manifest hash, reference-manifest hash, metric-suite hash, semantic metric id, bias metric id, hallucination or faithfulness metric id, judge-config hash, report hash, semantic similarity, bias risk, hallucination/faithfulness rate, signed evidence refs, and row hashes. These fields are included in row hashes and let Watch fail closed when live LLM/RAG systems lose semantic similarity, increase bias risk, increase hallucination or faithfulness risk, lose proof coverage, or drift across suite/reference/metric/judge context while the generic score remains stable.

NoMIRACL-style multilingual RAG rows can bind live relevance evidence: benchmark id, source reference, repository snapshot, license, dataset, language manifest, qrels, passage pool, retrieval run, model route, generation trace, evaluation report, baseline/live result, alert policy, language, relevant/non-relevant subset, query id, passage set, subset judgment, relevance accuracy, abstention accuracy, hallucination rate, error rate, signed evidence refs, and row hashes. These fields are included in row hashes and let Watch fail closed when live multilingual RAG systems lose relevance accuracy, lose abstention on unanswerable retrieved passages, increase hallucination or error rates, lose source/language/subset/evidence proof, or drift across language, subset, dataset, qrels, passage, retrieval, model, or alert-policy context while the generic score remains stable.

SLDBench-style scaling-law discovery rows can bind scientific-discovery live evidence: benchmark id, paper/source reference, eval-run id, task id, task type, dataset manifest, train/test split hashes, source-experiment manifest, task/evolution/evaluator configs, model-route hash, program artifact, checkpoint trace, result report, formula family, extrapolation regime, R2, NMSE, NMAE, signed evidence refs, and row hashes. These fields are included in row hashes and let Watch fail closed when live scaling-law discovery loses R2, increases NMSE or NMAE, loses proof coverage, or drifts across task type or benchmark/dataset/split/config/model/formula/extrapolation context while the generic score remains stable.
